{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\eta}/P_{\eta}))=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V}(1-c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)))^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{-}|\Sigma|.

The expression (
1.4.6
) for the number of critical points in a Lefschetz pencil is established by Katz in
[
SGA73
, Exposé XVII, cor. 5.6]
.
14
14

14

In
loc. cit.
, the right-hand side of (
1.4.6
) is shown to be the product of the degree of the Gauss map and of the degree of the dual hypersurface associated to
V
.

1.5.
Griffiths heights and Calabi-Yau manifolds

We conclude this introduction by some observations about the special forms taken by our formulas concerning Griffiths heights when applied to Calabi-Yau manifolds. This sheds some light on the significance of the assumption of being Calabi-Yau in the investigation of BCOV invariants, which have already been mentioned in
1.1.4
above.

1.5.1.
Pencils of Calabi-Yau manifolds

Let us consider a pencil of projective varieties:

g:Y\longrightarrow C

as in Theorems
1.3.1
and
1.3.2
, and assume that the smooth fibers of the morphism
g
are Calabi-Yau manifolds. In other terms, for every point
x
in
C-\Delta
, we assume that the canonical line bundle:

\det\Omega^{1}_{Y_{x}}\simeq(\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C})_{\mid Y_{x}}

of the fiber
Y_{x}:=g^{-1}(x)
is trivial.

The coherent sheaf
g_{\ast}\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
is torsion free on
C
, hence locally free. Over
C-\Delta
, it coincides with the relative Hodge bundle
H^{N-1,0}(Y_{C-\Delta}/C-\Delta)
, and therefore it defines a line bundle over
C
that we shall denote by:

L:=g_{\ast}\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C}.

The tautological “evaluation morphism” between line bundles over
Y
:

g^{\ast}L\longrightarrow\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C}

is an isomorphism on
Y-Y_{\Delta}
. If we denote by
V
the divisor in
Y
defined by the vanishing of this morphism, it defines an isomorphism:

(1.5.1)

g^{\ast}L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C})(-V).

From the isomorphism (
1.5.1
), we deduce an equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(Y)
:

(1.5.2)

c_{1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})=g^{\ast}c_{1}(L)+[V].

Moreover the support of the divisor
V
is contained in the support of
Y_{\Delta}
, and the divisor
V
can be written:

(1.5.3)

V=\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}D_{i},

where for every
i
in
I
,
v_{i}
is an integer.

Using (
1.5.2
) and (
1.5.3
), we obtain the equality of integers:

\displaystyle\int_{Y}c_{1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})\,c_{N-1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})

\displaystyle=\int_{Y}(g^{\ast}c_{1}(L)+[V])\,c_{N-1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C}),

\displaystyle=\int_{Y}g^{\ast}c_{1}(L)\,c_{N-1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})+\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}\int_{D_{i}}c_{N-1}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C|D_{i}}),

(1.5.4)

\displaystyle=(\deg L)\int_{Y_{\eta}}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1}_{Y_{\eta}})+\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}\int_{D_{i}}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2})),

(1.5.5)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N-1}(\deg L)\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Y_{\eta})+(-1)^{N-1}\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}),

where in (
1.5.4
), we have used Proposition
3.1.5
below, and in (
1.5.5
),
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Y_{\eta})
and
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})
denote respectively the Euler characteristic of a general fiber of the morphism
g
and of
{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}
.
15
15

15

See for instance Theorem
4.2.2
below for the equality:
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})=(-1)^{N-1}\int_{D_{i}}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2})).

Consequently, inserting (
1.5.5
) in the right-hand side of the equality (
1.3.5
) in Theorem
1.3.2
, we obtain the following expression for the alternating sum of the Griffiths heights of a pencil of Calabi-Yau manifolds, which may be seen as a geometric version of the BCOV invariant:

(1.5.6)

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))=-\frac{1}{12}\deg(g_{\ast}\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C})\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Y_{\eta})+\sum_{x\in\Delta}\beta_{x},

where, for every
x
in
\Delta
,
\beta_{x}
is the rational number given by:

\beta_{x}=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}[3(N-1)(m_{i}-1)-v_{i}]\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},i\prec j}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}).

Observe that, in the right-hand side of (
1.5.6
), the first term only depends on the general fiber of
Y
and on the line bundle “of modular forms”
L:=g_{\ast}\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
, and the second term only depends on the geometry of an infinitesimal neighborhood of the singular fibers.

When
N=3
and when the smooth fibers of
g
are K3 surfaces, and when the singular fibers are semistable
16
16

16

namely, when all the multiplicities
m_{i}
equal 1.
with smooth components, formula (
1.5.6
)
becomes:

(1.5.7)

{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK}(\mathbb{H}^{2}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))=2\deg g_{\ast}\omega_{Y/C}-\frac{1}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(D^{2}-D^{3})+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}),

where:

\omega_{Y/C}\simeq\det\omega^{1}_{Y/C}

denotes the relative dualizing line bundle of the morphism
g
.

Indeed, in this situation, the Griffiths heights in the left-hand side of (
1.5.6
) vanish if
n\neq 2,
and:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Y_{\eta})=24;

see for instance
[
Huy16
]
. One may actually expect to obtain a more explicit expression of the last two terms in the right-hand side of (
1.5.7
) when the degenerations of
Y/C
are Kulikov models; see
[
Huy16
, Chapter 6, §5]
.

1.5.2.
Lefschetz pencils on a Calabi-Yau manifold embedded in a projective space

Let us adopt the notation of Corollary
1.4.5
, and let us assume that
V
is a Calabi-Yau manifold, namely that the line bundle
\det\Omega^{1}_{V}
on
V
is trivial.

Under this assumption, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(V)
:

c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})=0,

and using it in equalities (
1.4.7
) and (
1.4.8
) of Corollary
1.4.5
yields the following expressions for the Griffiths heights of
the middle-dimensional cohomology of a Lefschetz pencil on a Calabi-Yau manifold:

{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\eta}/P_{\eta}))=\frac{(-1)^{N-1}}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{+}|\Sigma|,

and:

{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\eta}/P_{\eta}))=\frac{(-1)^{N-1}}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{-}|\Sigma|.

In particular, these Griffiths heights only depend on some basic invariants of
V
and its Lefschetz pencil, namely the Euler characteristic of
V
and the number of critical points of the pencil.

Chapter 2
Variations of Hodge structures associated to pencils of complete varieties with SNC degenerations: Steenbrink’s theory and elementary exponents

2.1.
Steenbrink’s theory and logarithmic Hodge bundles

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve, let
\Delta\subset C
be a finite subset, and let:

{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=C-\Delta,

be its complement in
C
. Let also
Y
be a smooth complex manifold, and let:

g:Y\longrightarrow C

be a proper complex analytic map, that is relatively projective, locally (in the analytic topology) over
C
, and such that the complex analytic map

g_{|Y_{{\mathaccent 23{C}}}}:Y_{{\mathaccent 23{C}}}:=g^{-1}({\mathaccent 23{C}})\longrightarrow{\mathaccent 23{C}}

is smooth (that
is, a complex analytic submersion).

Furthermore, let us assume that the divisor

Y_{\Delta}:=g^{\ast}\Delta

is a (not necessarily reduced) divisor with strict normal crossings, i.e. it can be written as
\sum\limits_{i}a_{i}D_{i}
, where the
(D_{i})_{i}
are smooth distinct divisors that intersect transversally and where the
(a_{i})_{i}
are positive integers.

Definition 2.1.1

(see for instance
[
Kat71
]
)
.

Let
\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log Y_{\Delta})
be the (analytic) sheaf of differentials with logarithmic singularities.
The sheaf on
Y
:

\Omega^{1}_{Y/C}(\log Y_{\Delta}):=\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log Y_{\Delta})/\mathrm{Im}(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta)\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log Y_{\Delta}))

is called the
sheaf of relative differentials with logarithmic singularities
. By taking local coordinates near a point of
Y_{\Delta}
, one can check that this is a locally free sheaf. Its rank on any connected component of
Y
is precisely the relative dimension of
g
on that component.

We also define the
relative logarithmic de Rham complex
:

\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}(\log Y_{\Delta}):=\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{\bullet}\Omega^{1}_{Y/C}(\log Y_{\Delta}),

with the differential induced by the one on the complex
j_{\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}
by restriction and quotient, where

j:Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}=Y-Y_{\Delta}\longrightarrow Y

denotes the inclusion morphism.

The
relative logarithmic de Rham cohomology sheaf
in degree
n
is defined as the analytic coherent sheaf on
C
:

{\mathcal{H}}^{n}_{\log}(Y/C):=R^{n}g_{\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}(\log Y_{\Delta}).

For simplicity’s sake, we will write
\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
for
\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}(\log Y_{\Delta})
as in
[
Kat89
]
and
[
Ill94
]
. Note that these sheaves do not depend on the choice of
\Delta
provided that
g
is smooth over
C-\Delta
; non-singular fibers in
Y_{\Delta}
would not contribute to them.

Also note that we have a relative logarithmic Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence:

(2.1.1)

E_{1}^{p,q}:=R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}\Rightarrow R^{p+q}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}.

The restriction to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
of this spectral sequence is the classical Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence of the smooth morphism:

g_{|Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}:Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathaccent 23{C}}.

This sequence degenerates at the first page (see for instance
[
Del68
, Th. (5.5)]
).

Consequently, for every integer
n
, there is a filtration
({\mathcal{F}}^{p})_{0\leq p\leq n}
of the vector bundle:

{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}}):=R^{n}g_{\mid Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}}}\simeq R^{n}g_{|Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}\ast}\mathbb{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathaccent 23{C}}

on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
, such that for every integer
0\leq p\leq n
, the subquotient
{\mathcal{F}}^{p}/{\mathcal{F}}^{p+1}
is isomorphic to

R^{n-p}g_{|Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}\Omega^{p}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}}},

and is locally free.

Finally, applying the functor
g_{\ast}
to the short exact sequence of complexes of sheaves on
Y
:

0\longrightarrow g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta)\otimes\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}[-1]\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}(\log Y_{\Delta})\longrightarrow\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}\longrightarrow 0,

we obtain a long exact sequence whose connecting homomorphism defines a logarithmic connection:

\nabla_{\log}:R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}\longrightarrow R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{C}}\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta),

whose restriction to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is the Gauss-Manin connection.

The following results are simple consequences of Steenbrink’s theory (see
[
Ste76
,
Ste77
]
).

Proposition 2.1.2
.

For all
n\geq 0
, the lower Deligne extension to
C
of the relative cohomology bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
with the Gauss-Manin connection is precisely the relative logarithmic de Rham cohomology
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}_{\log}(Y/C):=R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
equipped with the logarithmic connection
\nabla_{\log}
. In particular
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}_{\log}(Y/C)
is locally free over
C
.

Also, denoting by
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
the multiplicities of the components of
Y_{\Delta}
, the eigenvalues of the residue of the Gauss-Manin connection are in the set
\{\frac{j_{i}}{a_{i}}\;|\,i\in I,\,0\leq j_{i}\leq a_{i}-1\}
, and the (counterclockwise) monodromy endomorphism at every point of
\Delta
is quasi-unipotent.

Proof.

By
[
Ste76
, Th. (2.18)]
, for all
n\geq 0
, the sheaf
R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
is locally free on
C
.

By
[
Kat71
, VII]
(applied to the bundle
M:={\mathcal{O}}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}
equipped with the connection given by the differential
d
, and to the trivial extension
\overline{M}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
equipped with the differential
d
), we obtain that the only possible eigenvalues of the residue in
\Delta
of the logarithmic connection
\nabla_{\log}
are in the set
\{\frac{j_{i}}{a_{i}}\;|\,i\in I,\,0\leq j_{i}\leq a_{i}-1\}
. In particular, all the eigenvalues have their real part in
[0,1[
.

Using the definition of the lower Deligne extension and the fact that the (counterclockwise) monodromy in a point
x\in\Delta
is given by:

T_{x}=\exp(-2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm Res}_{x}\nabla_{\log}),

this gives the result.
∎

Proposition 2.1.3
.

The relative logarithmic Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence (
2.1.1
) degenerates at the first page, and for all
p,q\geq 0
, the sheaves
R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}
are locally free on
C
. Consequently, for all
(p,n)
such that
0\leq p\leq n
, the
p
-th subquotient of the Hodge filtration
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\log}
on the relative cohomology bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}_{\log}(Y/C)
given by the spectral sequence (
2.1.1
)satisfies:

\mathrm{gr}^{p}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\log}}(R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C})=R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}.

In particular, the vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}^{p}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
are algebraic vector subbundles of
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{{\mathaccent 23{C}}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
endowed with the algebraic structure defined by means of its Deligne extension.
1
1

1

This also follows from
[
Sch73
, Theorem (4.13), a)]
. Observe that the algebraic structures defined by the upper and lower Deligne extensions of a vector bundle with connection on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
actually coincide.

Proof.

By
[
Ste77
, Th. (2.11)]
, for all
p,q\geq 0
, the sheaves
E_{1}^{p,q}:=R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}
are locally free.

The restriction of the spectral sequence (
2.1.1
) to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is the classical relative Hodge-de Rham sequence, which degenerates at the first page. This means that the differentials of the sequence
(
2.1.1
)
\mathrm{d}_{1}^{p,q}:E_{1}^{p,q}\rightarrow E_{1}^{p+1,q}
vanish on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
, hence everywhere by local freeness of the sheaves
E_{1}^{p,q}
. So the relative logarithmic Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence degenerates at the first page.

The second statement follows by definition of the spectral sequence.

For the third one, from the GAGA theorem applied on the projective manifold
C
, we have that the analytic vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}^{p}_{\log}
are algebraic subbundles of the Deligne extension
R^{n}g_{*}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
of
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
, hence their restrictions to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
are algebraic with respect to the same algebraic structure.
∎

Proposition 2.1.4
.

For every integer
n\geq 0
, letting
\eta
be the generic point of
C
, the lower Griffiths line bundle of the relative cohomology VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
exists and is given by:

\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\simeq\bigotimes_{0\leq p\leq n}(\det R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})^{\otimes p}.

Proof.

Let
n
be a non-negative integer.
Using Proposition
2.1.2
, the lower Deligne extension of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
equipped with the Gauss-Manin connection coincides with the relative logarithmic cohomology
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}_{\log}(Y/C)\simeq R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
equipped with the logarithmic connection
\nabla_{\log}
.

Using Proposition
2.1.3
, for every integer
p\geq 0
, the vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}^{p}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is an algebraic subbundle of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
endowed with the algebraic structure defined by
R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
, and its extension in the vector bundle
R^{n}g_{\ast}\omega^{\bullet}_{Y/C}
is the vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}^{p}_{\log}
given by the logarithmic Hodge-de Rham exact sequence.

Consequently, the lower Griffiths line bundle is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle\simeq\bigotimes_{p\geq 0}(\det{\mathcal{F}}^{p}_{\log}/{\mathcal{F}}^{p+1}_{\log})^{\otimes p},

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{0\leq p\leq n}(\det R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})^{\otimes p}\textrm{ using Proposition \ref{logarithmic Hodge bundles}}.\qed

2.2.
Elementary exponents of a degeneration

In this section, we recall the formalism of the elementary exponents of a normal crossing degeneration, as developed in
[
EFiMM21
, Sections 2.2 and 2.3]
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be the unit complex disk with coordinate
t
, let
Y
be a smooth complex manifold, and let

g:Y\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}

be a projective morphism such that the fiber
Y_{0}
is a (not necessarily reduced) divisor with strict normal crossings.

Let us consider a
semistable reduction diagram
, i.e. a commutative diagram of complex manifolds

(2.2.1)

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

where
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
is a copy of the unit open disk with coordinate
t^{\prime}
,
\sigma
is the map sending
t^{\prime}
to
t^{\prime l}
where
l
is some integer,
Y^{\prime}
is a smooth
N
-dimensional complex manifold,
g^{\prime}
is a morphism such that the open subscheme
Y^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}_{0}
of
Y^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the fiber product
(Y-Y_{0})\times_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}
, and such that
Y^{\prime}_{0}
is a
reduced
divisor with strict normal crossings.

Such a diagram exists by the “semistable reduction theorem” (
[
KKMSD73
, Chapter II]
).

For every pair of integers
(p,q)
, we can consider the coherent sheaf
R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}
(resp.
R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}
) on
\mathbb{D}
(resp.
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
), which is locally free using Proposition
2.1.3
. Both locally free sheaves have the same rank, given by the Hodge number of a general fiber:

h^{p,q}:=h^{p,q}(Y_{\infty}).

Definition 2.2.1

(
[
EFiMM21
, Def. 2.5]
)
.

Let
p,q\geq 0
be integers. With the above notation, the map of locally free sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
given by the pullback of forms:

\sigma^{\ast}R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}

induces an isomorphism on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}
, so it is injective and its cokernel is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}
-module of the form

\bigoplus_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}/(t^{\prime}{}^{b^{p,q}_{j}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}),

where
(b^{p,q}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}
is a family of integers, unique up to order.

The
elementary exponents of the
(p,q)
-Hodge bundle
are the rational numbers:

(\alpha^{p,q}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}:=\left(\frac{b^{p,q}_{j}}{l}\right)_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}\quad\in\left(\frac{1}{l}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{h^{p,q}}.

We also define rational numbers by:

b^{p,q}:=\sum_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}b^{p,q}_{j},

\alpha^{p,q}:=\sum_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}\alpha^{p,q}_{j}=\frac{b^{p,q}}{l}.

Note that these numbers do not depend on the choice of the coordinates
t^{\prime}
,
t
.

Proposition 2.2.2

(
[
EFiMM21
, Lemma 2.4]
)
.

For all integers
p,q
, the integers
(b_{j}^{p,q})_{j}
are all in
\{0,...,l-1\}
. Consequently, the elementary exponents
(\alpha_{j}^{p,q})_{j}
are all in
\{0,\frac{1}{l},...,\frac{l-1}{l}\}
.

Moreover, the elementary exponents do not depend on the choice of the semistable reduction diagram.

The elementary exponents are related to the eigenvalues of the (counterclockwise) monodromy.

Let
\infty
be a point in
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}
, and
n\geq 0
be an integer. As in
[
Ste77
, (2.8)]
, we can equip the fiber

(R^{n}g_{\ast}\mathbb{C})_{\infty}\simeq H^{n}(Y_{\infty},\mathbb{C})

with a mixed Hodge structure. Let us denote by
F_{\infty}^{\bullet}
the associated Hodge filtration, not to be confused with the classical Hodge filtration
({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet})_{\infty}
on the same space.

Using
[
Ste77
, Th. (2.13)]
, the semisimple part of the monodromy:

T_{s}:H^{n}(Y_{\infty})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{n}(Y_{\infty})

respects the mixed Hodge structure, hence the filtration
F_{\infty}^{\bullet}
.

Proposition 2.2.3

(
[
EFiMM21
, Cor. 2.8]
)
.

For every pair of integers
(p,q)
with
p+q=n
, letting
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j})_{j}
be the elementary exponents of the
(p,q)
-Hodge bundle, the complex numbers

(\exp(-2i\pi\alpha^{p,q}_{j}))_{j}

are precisely the eigenvalues (counted with multiplicities) of the endomorphism
T_{s}
acting on the subquotient
F_{\infty}^{p}/F_{\infty}^{p+1}
.

This proposition yields a new proof that the elementary exponents do not depend on the choice of the semistable reduction diagram.

We can also use them to compare the determinant line bundles of the Hodge bundles.

Proposition 2.2.4
.

With the same notation, the canonical isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det R^{q}g_{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{Y-Y_{0}/\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}\longrightarrow\det R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}_{0}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}

can be extended into an isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

(\sigma^{\ast}\det R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}(\{0\})^{\otimes l\alpha^{p,q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\det R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}.

Proof.

This follows from the multiplicativity of the determinant line bundle applied to the exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

0\longrightarrow\sigma^{\ast}R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{1\leq j\leq h^{p,q}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}/(t^{\prime l\alpha^{p,q}_{j}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}})\longrightarrow 0.\qed

2.3.
Comparison of Griffiths line bundles and elementary exponents

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
,
\Delta
be a finite subset of
C
, which we also see as a reduced divisor,
Y
be a smooth
N
-dimensional complex scheme, and

g:Y\longrightarrow C

be a projective morphism which is smooth over
{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=C-\Delta
, and such that
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings. As before, we shall denote:

Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=g^{-1}({\mathaccent 23{C}})=Y-Y_{\Delta}.

For every point
x
in
\Delta
, let
l_{x}
be an integer such that there exists an open disk
\mathbb{D}
centered in
x
with a coordinate
t
that identifies it with the unit open disk, such that the restriction

g_{\mathbb{D}}:Y_{\mathbb{D}}:=g^{-1}(\mathbb{D})\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}

and the morphism

\sigma:\mathbb{D^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{D},\quad t^{\prime}\longrightarrow t^{\prime l_{x}}

where
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
is a copy of the unit open disk with coordinate
t^{\prime}
, can be inserted into a semistable reduction diagram of the form (
2.2.1
).

Let
C^{\prime}
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta^{\prime}
, and

\sigma:C^{\prime}\longrightarrow C

be a finite morphism satisfying the following condition:

(2.3.1)

For every
x^{\prime}
in
\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)
, the ramification index
r_{x^{\prime}}
of
\sigma
in
x^{\prime}
is a multiple of the integer
l_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}
.

In particular, for every integer
n
, the monodromy of the bundle
\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
with the connection given by the pullback of the Gauss-Manin connection, is unipotent at every point of
\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)
.

For
n
an integer, let
{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
be the vector bundle with connection
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
, and
F^{\bullet}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
be its Hodge filtration.

Let
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}
) be its upper (resp. lower) Deligne extension to
C
.

We know that the vector bundle with connection
\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
on:

{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}:=C^{\prime}-\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)

has unipotent local monodromy at the points of
\Delta^{\prime}:=\sigma^{-1}(\Delta),
so we can consider its Deligne extension
\overline{\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
to
C^{\prime}
.

Using Proposition
2.1.3
, for every integer
p
, the vector subbundle
F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is an algebraic subbundle of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
endowed with the algebraic structure defined by means of its lower Deligne extension
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}
. As the algebraic structures defined by the upper and the lower Deligne extensions actually coincide, the vector subbundle
F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is also an algebraic subbundle of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
endowed with the algebraic structure defined by its upper Deligne extension
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}
. Consequently, we can consider the extension
\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}
(resp.
\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}
) in the vector bundle
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}
) of the vector subbundle
F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
in
{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
.

Similarly, we can consider the extension
\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
in
\overline{\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
, over
C^{\prime}
, of the subbundle
\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
in
\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
over
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

For all integers
p,q
, and for every
x
in
\Delta
, let
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{1\leq j\leq\mathrm{rg}(H^{p,q}(Y_{\eta}))}
be the elementary exponents of the
(p,q)
-Hodge bundle at point
x
, and let
\alpha^{p,q}_{x}
be their sum.

Let us define a divisor on
C
with rational coefficients by:

A^{p,q}:=\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha^{p,q}_{x}\{x\}.

Proposition 2.3.1
.

With the above notation, for all integers
p
,
n
, there is an isomorphism of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}A^{p,n-p})\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}})

whose restriction to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
is the identity of the line bundle
\sigma^{\ast}\det(F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}/F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n})
.

Note that even though
A^{p,n-p}
is a divisor with only rational coefficients, its pullback by
\sigma
necessarily has integral coefficients.

Proof.

It is enough to define these isomorphisms locally around a point
x^{\prime}
in
\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)
. Let
r_{x^{\prime}}
be the ramification index of
\sigma
at
x^{\prime}
, and
x
be the image of
x^{\prime}
by
\sigma
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be an open disk centered in
x
with a coordinate
t
that identifies
\mathbb{D}
with the unit open disk, and let
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
be an open disk around
x^{\prime}
with a coordinate
t^{\prime}
that identifies
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
with the unit open disk, such that
\sigma(\mathbb{D}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathbb{D}
and that we have the equality of functions on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}t=t^{\prime r_{x^{\prime}}}.

Using condition (
2.3.1
), the integer
r_{x^{\prime}}
can be written
r^{\prime}l_{x}
, where
r^{\prime}\geq 1
and
l_{x}
is an integer such that we have a semistable reduction diagram

\textstyle{(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathbb{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

where
\sigma^{\prime}
is the elevation to the power
l_{x}
.

Let

\tau:\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}

be the elevation to the power
r^{\prime}
, so that we have the equality of morphisms:

\sigma^{\prime}\circ\tau=\sigma_{|\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}.

Using Proposition
2.1.2
, and the fact that
(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}
is semistable, i.e. that the fiber
(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{0}
is a reduced divisor with strict normal crossings, we obtain that for every
n\geq 0
, the monodromy of the vector bundle
R^{n}(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{\mid(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}-(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{0}\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}-(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{0}/\mathbb{D^{\prime\prime}}-\{0\}}
, with the Gauss-Manin connection, is unipotent. Consequently, the upper and lower Deligne extensions on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}
coincide and are compatible with base change by ramified morphisms.

Consequently, the extensions of the subquotients in the lower Deligne extensions are also compatible with base change. So using Proposition
2.1.3
, we obtain, for all integers
p,n
, an isomorphism of vector bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

(2.3.2)

\tau^{\ast}R^{n-p}(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}}\simeq\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}-\{x^{\prime}\}
.

Using Proposition
2.2.4
, we have an isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}
:

(\sigma^{\prime\ast}\det R^{n-p}g_{\mathbb{D}\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y_{\mathbb{D}}/\mathbb{D}})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}}(\{0\})^{\otimes l_{x}\alpha^{p,n-p}_{x}}\simeq\det R^{n-p}(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}},

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}-\{0\}
. Pulling it back by
\tau
and using the isomorphism (
2.3.2
), we get an isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\ast}\det R^{n-p}g_{\mathbb{D}\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y_{\mathbb{D}}/\mathbb{D}})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}(\{x^{\prime}\})^{\otimes r_{x^{\prime}}\alpha^{p,n-p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\simeq\tau^{\ast}\det R^{n-p}(g_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{(Y_{\mathbb{D}})^{\prime\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}).

Using again Proposition
2.1.3
, this can be rewritten as an isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}(\{x^{\prime}\})^{\otimes r_{x^{\prime}}\alpha^{p,n-p}_{x}}\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}})

that extends the identity map on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}-\{x^{\prime}\}
. Using this extension for every
x^{\prime}
in
\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)
shows that there is an isomorphism of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

(\sigma^{\ast}\det\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)}r_{x^{\prime}}\alpha^{p,n-p}_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}\{x^{\prime}\}\right)\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}).

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

The wanted isomorphism follows, using the fact that for every
x^{\prime}
in
\sigma^{-1}(\Delta)
, the multiplicity of the divisor
\sigma^{\ast}A^{p,n-p}
at
x^{\prime}
is exactly
r_{x^{\prime}}\alpha^{p,n-p}_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}
(which is an integer because the rational number
\alpha^{p,n-p}_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}
is in
\frac{1}{l_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}}\mathbb{Z}
by definition, and because the integer
r_{x^{\prime}}
is a multiple of
l_{\sigma(x^{\prime})}
by condition (
2.3.1
)).
∎

Proposition 2.3.2
.

For every pair
(p,n)
of integers such that
0\leq p\leq n,
there is an isomorphism of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}(-\sigma^{\ast}A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)})\longrightarrow\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}})

whose restriction to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
is the identity of the line bundle
\sigma^{\ast}\det(F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}/F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n})
.

Proof.

Using Poincaré and Serre duality, for every integer
n
, the vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}^{n}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is isomorphic to the dual of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}
, and for every integer
p
, the subquotient:

F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}/F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}\simeq R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}

is identified to the dual of the subquotient

F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}/F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}\simeq R^{(N-1)-(n-p)}g_{\ast}\Omega^{(N-1)-p}_{Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}}.

As the duality exchanges the upper and lower Deligne extensions, we obtain an isomorphism of vector bundles on
C
:

\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}\simeq(\overline{F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-})^{\vee}

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
; hence, pulling back by
\sigma
and taking the determinant line bundle, an isomorphism of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+})\simeq\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-})^{\vee}

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

Applying Proposition
2.3.1
to the integers
p^{\prime}:=(N-1)-p
,
n^{\prime}:=2(N-1)-n
, we obtain an isomorphism:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{\ast}A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)})

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}})

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
; and consequently, an isomorphism:

\displaystyle\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(-\sigma^{\ast}A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)})

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}})^{\vee}

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

Applying Poincaré and Serre duality again to the vector bundle
\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
and its subquotient
\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}/\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}
, and using that duality sends the (upper or lower) Deligne extension on
C^{\prime}
of this subquotient to the (upper or lower) Deligne extension of its dual, we obtain an isomorphism:

(\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{(N-1)-p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{2(N-1)-n}})^{\vee}\simeq\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}/\overline{\sigma^{\ast}F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}},

that extends the canonical isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

This shows the result.
∎

Proposition 2.3.3
.

With the same notation, for all integers
p,n
,
there is an isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)})\longrightarrow\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+})

whose restriction to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is the identity of the line bundle
\det(F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}/F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n})
.

In particular, the coefficients of the divisor
A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}
are integers.

Proof.

Combining the isomorphisms of Propositions
2.3.1
and
2.3.2
yields an isomorphism of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{-})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}(A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}))\simeq\sigma^{\ast}\det(\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+}/\overline{F^{p+1}{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}_{+})

that extends the identity isomorphism on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}^{\prime}
.

This isomorphism, restricted to
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
, is exactly the pullback by
\sigma
of the identity isomorphism, so that the latter has an extension to an isomorphism on
C
as wanted.
∎

Now, we can apply these results to compute Griffiths bundles.

Proposition 2.3.4
.

For every integer
n\geq 0
, we have isomorphisms of line bundles on
C^{\prime}
:

(2.3.3)

\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\,\sigma^{\ast}A^{p,n-p}\right)\simeq\mathcal{GK}_{C^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})),

(2.3.4)

\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}\left(-\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\,\sigma^{\ast}A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}\right)\simeq\mathcal{GK}_{C^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})),

and an isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

(2.3.5)

\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}\left(\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p(A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)})\right)\simeq\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})).

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
2.3.1
,
2.3.2
and
2.3.3
and the definition of Griffiths bundles.
∎

Corollary 2.3.5
.

For every
n\geq 0
, we have the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)
:

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p([A^{p,n-p}]+[A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}]),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}pc_{1}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p([A^{p,n-p}]+[A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}]),

hence the equalities of integers:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle={\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}+A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)});

and we also have the equalities of rational numbers:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,stab}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle={\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}).

Proof.

The first four equalities follow from the isomorphism (
2.3.5
) in Proposition
2.3.4
and Proposition
2.1.4
.

For the last two equalities, by definition of the stable height, using the isomorphism (
2.3.3
) in Proposition
2.3.4
and Proposition
2.1.4
, we have the equalities of rational numbers:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,stab}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\deg(\sigma)}\deg_{C^{\prime}}(\mathcal{GK}_{C^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\deg(\sigma)}\deg_{C^{\prime}}\left(\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\,\sigma^{\ast}A^{p,n-p}\right)\right),

\displaystyle=\deg_{C}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(A^{p,n-p}).\qed

2.4.
Comparison of Griffiths line bundles in the case of non-degenerate critical points

2.4.1.
Non-degenerate critical points and elementary exponents

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve, let
H
be an
N
-dimensional smooth projective complex manifold, and let:

f:H\longrightarrow C

be a complex analytic morphism whose only critical points are non-degenerate.
2
2

2

Namely. at every point where the differential of
f
vanishes, its Hessian is non-degenerate.
Let
\Sigma
be the set of these critical points and
\Delta:=f(\Sigma)
be its set-theoretic image by
f
.

We can rephrase the hypothesis as the divisor in
H
:

H_{\Delta}:=f^{\ast}\Delta

having only finitely many singularities, all of which are ordinary double points.

Let

\nu:Y:=\tilde{H}\longrightarrow H

be the blow-up of these points and

g:=f\circ\nu:Y\longrightarrow C

be the composition. For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, let us define a subscheme in
Y
by:

E_{P}:=\nu^{-1}(\{P\}),

so that the
(E_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
are the connected components of the exceptional divisor
E
. In particular, they are disjoint divisors isomorphic to the complex projective space
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
.

Let us define a divisor in
Y
by:

Y_{\Delta}:=g^{\ast}\Delta=\nu^{\ast}H_{\Delta}.

Since the divisor
H_{\Delta}
has its only singularities at points of
\Sigma
, and since all of these singularities are ordinary double points, its pullback divisor
Y_{\Delta}
can be written as follows:

(2.4.1)

Y_{\Delta}=2\sum_{P\in\Sigma}E_{P}+W

where
W
is the proper transform in
Y
of the divisor
H_{\Delta}
in
H
: it is a non-singular divisor intersecting transversally the components
(E_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
of the exceptional divisor, and for every point
P
, the intersection
E_{P}\cap W
is a smooth quadric in the projective space
E_{P}
.

In particular, the divisor
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings.

This section is devoted to the proof of the following result.

Proposition 2.4.1
.

With the above notation, for every
x
in
\Delta
and
p,q
integers, all the elementary exponents
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{j}
of the degeneration
g
at point
x
vanish unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=\frac{N-1}{2}
.

If
N-1
is even and
p=q=\frac{N-1}{2}
, precisely
|\Sigma_{x}|
of the
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{j}
are equal to
\frac{1}{2}
, and the other ones vanish.

In particular, the rational number
\alpha^{p,q}_{x}
vanishes unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=\frac{N-1}{2}
, in which case it is equal to
\frac{1}{2}|\Sigma_{x}|
.

In other terms, the divisor
A^{p,q}
with rational coefficients in
C
vanishes unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=\frac{N-1}{2}
, in which case it is given by:

A^{\frac{N-1}{2},\frac{N-1}{2}}=\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma].

This proposition is stated in
[
EFiMM21
, Prop. 3.10]
, but only proved there in the case where each fiber of the morphism
f
contains only one critical point. In Subsection
2.4.2
below, we present an argument, which was explained to us by the authors of
[
EFiMM21
]
, that reduces the proposition to this special case.

Corollary 2.4.2
.

For every integer n, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(2.4.2)

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

(2.4.3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}pc_{1}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

hence the equality of rational numbers:

(2.4.4)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle={\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}|\Sigma|

(2.4.5)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}|\Sigma|;

and we also have the equality of rational numbers:

(2.4.6)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,stab}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle={\mathrm{ht}}_{GK,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{4}|\Sigma|

(2.4.7)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p\deg_{C}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C})+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{4}|\Sigma|,

where
\eta_{N}
is
1
if
N
is odd and
0
if
N
is even.

Proof.

For
n
an integer, using Proposition
2.4.1
, the sum in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p([A^{p,n-p}]+[A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}])

vanishes unless
n=N-1
and
N-1
is even, and in this case, it is equal to

\frac{N-1}{2}([A^{\frac{N-1}{2},\frac{N-1}{2}}]+[A^{\frac{N-1}{2},\frac{N-1}{2}}])=\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma].

So for every integer
n
, we obtain the equality:

\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p([A^{p,n-p}]+[A^{(N-1)-p,(N-1)-(n-p)}])=\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma].

Similarly, for every integer
n
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}p[A^{p,n-p}]=\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{4}f_{\ast}[\Sigma].

Replacing these equalities in Corollary
2.3.5
yields the result.
∎

2.4.2.
Proof of Proposition
2.4.1

Let
x
be a point in
\Delta
. As the proposition is local around
x
, we can replace the curve
C
by the unit open disk
\mathbb{D}
with coordinate
t
, the point
x
by
0
, and assume that
f
is smooth over
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}:=\mathbb{D}-\{0\}
and
admits only non-degenerate critical points. In particular, these critical points are the points in
\Sigma_{x}
; we shall simply call this subset
\Sigma
.

Let us introduce a copy
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
of the unit open disk with coordinate
t^{\prime}
, let us define an analytic morphism by:

\sigma:\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{D},\quad t^{\prime}\longmapsto t^{\prime 2},

and let us define the fiber product:

(2.4.8)

\textstyle{\tilde{H}^{\prime}:=Y\times_{\mathbb{D}}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

Let us define the following divisors in the singular analytic space
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

E^{\prime}_{P}:=\rho^{\ast}E_{P},P\in\Sigma,

E^{\prime}:=\bigsqcup_{P\in\Sigma}E^{\prime}_{P},

and:

W^{\prime}:=\rho^{\ast}W.

Using (
2.4.1
), we get the equality of Cartier divisors in
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

(2.4.9)

2\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}=\rho^{\ast}Y_{0}=2E^{\prime}+W^{\prime}.

Let us consider the normalization of the singular analytic space
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

\pi:Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow\tilde{H}^{\prime},

and the composition:

h:=g^{\prime}\circ\pi:Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}^{\prime}.

Since the morphism
g
is smooth on the open subset
Y-E\subset Y
, the morphism
g^{\prime}
is smooth on the open subset
\tilde{H}^{\prime}-E^{\prime}\subset\tilde{H}^{\prime}
, in particular the analytic space
\tilde{H}^{\prime}-E^{\prime}
is non-singular, and the morphism
\pi
induces an isomorphism between
Y^{\prime}-\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime})
and
\tilde{H}^{\prime}-E^{\prime}
.

The cokernel of the canonical injective morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
-modules

\varphi:{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}

is supported by the closed subset
E^{\prime}
, so we can write it as follows:

\mathrm{Coker}(\varphi)=\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{P},

where for every point
P
in
\Sigma
,
\mathcal{F}_{P}
is a coherent sheaf supported by the closed subset
E^{\prime}_{P}
.

The proof of Proposition
2.4.1
shall rely on the following three lemmas.

Lemma 2.4.3
.

The three following statements hold.

(i)
The morphism

h:Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}^{\prime}

defines a semistable reduction of the morphism
g
; namely the analytic space
Y^{\prime}
is non-singular and the fiber
Y^{\prime}_{0}:=h^{-1}(0)
is a reduced divisor with strict normal crossings in
Y^{\prime}
.

(ii)
For every integer
p
, the canonical isomorphism of vector bundles on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}
:

(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{Y_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}/\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}

extends into an isomorphism of vector bundles on
Y^{\prime}
:

(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}.

(iii)
For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
-module
(g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{P}
vanishes.

Proof.

The three statements can be proved in local models of
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
and of
Y^{\prime}
. We shall only show them in a local model of
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
near a point of
E^{\prime}_{P}\cap W^{\prime}
and a local model of
Y^{\prime}
near a point of
\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{P}\cap W^{\prime})
(where
P
is a point in
\Sigma
) when
N\geq 2
, which is the most complicated case.

Let
P
be a point in
\Sigma
. Near a point of
E_{P}\cap W
, we can choose a local coordinate system
(y_{1},...,y_{N})
of
Y
, such that the divisor
W
is defined by
(y_{1}=0)
, the divisor
E_{P}
is defined by
(y_{2}=0)
, and satisfying the equality of functions:

g^{\ast}t=y_{1}y_{2}^{2}.

Consequently, near a point of
E^{\prime}_{P}\cap W^{\prime}
, the analytic space
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
admits coordinates
(g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime},\rho^{\ast}y_{1},...,\rho^{\ast}y_{N})
, subjected to the relation:

g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime 2}=(\rho^{\ast}y_{1})\,(\rho^{\ast}y_{2})^{2}.

The local model is that of the product of a “Whitney umbrella” singularity by
\mathbb{C}^{N-2}
.

Finally, near a point of
\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{P}\cap W^{\prime})
, the analytic space
Y^{\prime}
admits a local coordinate system
(z,(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}y_{2},...,(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}y_{N})
satisfying the relations:

h^{\ast}t^{\prime}=z\,(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}y_{2}

and:

(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}y_{1}=z^{2}.

In particular, the analytic space
Y^{\prime}
is smooth, and the divisor
\pi^{\ast}W^{\prime}
in
Y^{\prime}
is of multiplicity
2
, which allows us to define a divisor by
(1/2)\pi^{\ast}W^{\prime}
(defined by
(z=0)
in the above local model). We also have that the morphism
h
is smooth over
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}
, and that the divisor
Y^{\prime}_{0}
in
Y^{\prime}
can be written:

(2.4.10)

Y^{\prime}_{0}=\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\pi^{\ast}E^{\prime}_{P}+(1/2)\pi^{\ast}W^{\prime},

where the divisors
(\pi^{\ast}E^{\prime}_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
are disjoint and non-singular, where the divisor
(1/2)\pi^{\ast}W^{\prime}
is non-singular, and intersects transversally the divisors
(\pi^{\ast}E^{\prime}_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
.

Consequently, the degeneration
h
is semistable, which shows (i).

It clearly suffices to prove (ii) when the integer
p
is
1
. In the above local models, the locally free sheaf
\omega^{1}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}
on
Y
is generated by
([\mathrm{d}y_{1}/y_{1}],[\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}],[\mathrm{d}y_{3}],...,[\mathrm{d}y_{N}])
modulo the relation
[\mathrm{d}y_{1}/y_{1}]+2[\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}]=0
, hence it admits a local frame
([\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}],[\mathrm{d}y_{3}],...,[\mathrm{d}y_{N}])
.

On the other hand, the locally free sheaf
\omega^{1}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}
on
Y^{\prime}
is generated by (
[\mathrm{d}z/z]
,
(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}[\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}]
,
(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}[\mathrm{d}y_{3}]
,…) modulo the relation
[\mathrm{d}z/z]+(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}[\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}]=0
, hence it admits a local frame
((\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}[\mathrm{d}y_{2}/y_{2}],(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}[\mathrm{d}y_{3}],...)
.

The canonical morphism of sheaves on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}
:

(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{Y_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}/\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}\simeq(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\omega^{1}_{Y/\mathbb{D}|Y_{\mathbb{D}^{\ast}}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y^{\prime}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}\simeq\omega^{1}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}|Y^{\prime}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}}}

is compatible with these local frames, hence extends into an isomorphism on
Y^{\prime}
, which shows (ii).

For (iii), in the above local models, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
-algebra
\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}
is generated by
z
, subjected to the relations:

g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime}=z\,(\rho^{\ast}y_{2})

and:

\rho^{\ast}y_{1}=z^{2}.

Consequently, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
-module
\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}
is generated by the powers of
z
.
For every even integer
r
, the element
z^{r}
is contained in
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
, so this module is generated by the odd powers of
z
.

Let
r
be an odd integer, we have the equality of elements of the algebra
\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}
:

(g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime})\,z^{r}=z^{r+1}\,(\rho^{\ast}y_{2})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}},

Consequently, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
-submodule
(g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime})\,\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}
of
\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}
is contained in
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}}
. By definition of the morphism
\varphi
, the element
(g^{\prime\ast}t^{\prime})
is in the annihilator of its cokernel
\mathcal{F}_{P}
, which shows (iii).
∎

Lemma 2.4.4
.

For every pair of non-negative integers
(p,q)
, the cokernel of the natural injective map of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

u^{p,q}:\sigma^{\ast}R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow R^{q}h_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}

is isomorphic to the coherent sheaf:

\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}).

Proof.

Tensoring the following exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{P}\longrightarrow 0,

by the locally free sheaf
\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}
, and using the projection formula, yields an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

(2.4.11)

0\longrightarrow\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow\pi_{\ast}(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow 0.

Using Lemma
2.4.3
, (ii), we have a canonical isomorphism of vector bundles on
Y^{\prime}
:

(\rho\circ\pi)^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}.

Hence replacing in (
2.4.11
), we obtain an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
:

(2.4.12)

0\longrightarrow\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow\pi_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow 0.

The higher direct images of (
2.4.12
) by the morphism
g^{\prime}
fit into the following long exact sequence of coherent analytic sheaves over
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
(with the convention that
R^{-1}g^{\prime}_{\ast}=0
):

(2.4.13)

...\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q-1}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\pi_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}})\\
\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow R^{q+1}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow...

Since the morphism
\sigma
is flat, by base change using the commutative diagram (
2.4.8
), we obtain the following isomorphisms of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\simeq\sigma^{\ast}R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}},

and:

R^{q+1}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\simeq\sigma^{\ast}R^{q+1}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}.

These sheaves are locally free on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
by Proposition
2.1.3
.

On the other hand, since for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{P}
on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
is supported on
E^{\prime}_{P}
, the coherent sheaves
\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})
and
\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q-1}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})
on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
are clearly supported by the point
0
.

Consequently, the connecting morphisms in (
2.4.13
) vanish, and (
2.4.13
) induces a short exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

(2.4.14)

0\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\simeq\sigma^{\ast}R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}\longrightarrow R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\pi_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})\longrightarrow 0.

Furthermore, the normalization morphism
\pi
is finite, so its higher direct image functors on coherent sheaves vanish. Using the spectral sequence of composed functors, we can rewrite the second sheaf in (
2.4.14
):

R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\pi_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}})\simeq R^{q}h_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y^{\prime}/\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}.

Under this identification, the second arrow in the diagram (
2.4.14
) is precisely
u^{p,q}
. So we obtain that its cokernel is the coherent sheaf on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
given by:

\mathrm{Coker}\,u^{p,q}\simeq\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.4.5
.

The isomorphism class of the coherent sheaf on
{\mathbb{D}}^{\prime}
, supported by
\{0\}
:

R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}),

where
(p,q)
is a pair of non-negative integers and
P
is a point in
\Sigma
, only depends on the integers
N,

p,
and
q
.

This lemma is a simple consequence of the local and intrinsic character of the notion of non-degenerate critical point, and of the operations of blow-up, base change and normalization involved in the above construction. For the sake of completeness, we include some details that the readers could skip at their discretion.

Proof.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, we can construct local models of neighborhoods of
E^{\prime}_{P}
in
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
and of
\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{P})
in
Y^{\prime}
, that only depend on the integer
N
, as follows.

Since the morphism
f
admits a non-degenerate critical point at
P
, we can choose an analytic coordinate system
(x_{1,P},...,x_{N,P})
on a neighborhood
U_{P}
of
P
in
H
, defining a morphism inserted in a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{U_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f_{|U_{P}}}
\textstyle{U\subset\mathbb{C}^{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{loc}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

where
U
is some fixed neighborhood of
0
in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
(for instance the open polydisk of radius
\sqrt{1/N}
centered in 0), and
f_{loc}
is the morphism sending
(x_{1},...,x_{N})
to
x_{1}^{2}+...+x_{N}^{2}
.

Taking the blow-ups of
U_{P}
at
P
and of
U
at the origin, we obtain a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{V_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{g_{|V_{P}}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{loc}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

where
V_{P}
is a neighborhood of
E_{P}
in
\tilde{H}
,
V
is an analytic manifold that only depends on
N
, containing a divisor
E_{loc}
identified with
E_{P}
, and
g_{loc}
is an analytic morphism that only depends on
N
.

Taking the fiber product of the morphisms
g_{|V_{P}}
and
g_{loc}
with the morphism
\sigma
, then taking the normalizations of the resulting analytic spaces, we obtain a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{W_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{|W_{P}}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{loc}}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}_{|V^{\prime}_{P}}}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}_{loc}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{loc}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{loc}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

where
V^{\prime}_{P}
(resp.
W_{P}
) is a neighborhood of
E^{\prime}_{P}
(resp.
\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{P})
) in
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
(resp.
Y^{\prime}
),
V^{\prime}
(resp.
W
) is an analytic space (resp. manifold) that only depends on
N
, containing a divisor
E^{\prime}_{loc}
(resp.
\pi_{loc}^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{loc})
) identified with
E^{\prime}_{P}
(resp.
\pi^{-1}(E^{\prime}_{P})
) via the isomorphisms, and
\pi_{loc}
,
g^{\prime}_{loc}
, and
\rho_{loc}
are analytic morphisms that only depend on
N
.

The morphism of coherent sheaves
\varphi_{|V^{\prime}_{P}}
on
V^{\prime}_{P}
is identified via these isomorphisms with a morphism of coherent sheaves on
V^{\prime}
:

\varphi_{loc}:{\mathcal{O}}_{V^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\pi_{loc\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{loc}
be its cokernel: it is a coherent sheaf on
V^{\prime}
supported on
E^{\prime}_{loc}
, that only depends on
N
, identified via the isomorphisms with
\mathcal{F}_{P}
.

Similarly, for every integer
p
, the locally free sheaf
(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|V^{\prime}_{P}}
on
V^{\prime}_{P}
is identified with the locally free sheaf
\rho_{loc}^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{V/\mathbb{D}}
on
V^{\prime}
, which only depends on
N
.

Consequently, for every pair of integers
(p,q)
, it follows from the commutativity of the diagram, and from the fact that the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})
is supported on
E^{\prime}_{P}
, hence on
V^{\prime}_{P}
, that we have an isomorphism of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})

\displaystyle\simeq R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|V^{\prime}_{P}},

\displaystyle\simeq R^{q}g^{\prime}_{loc\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{loc}\otimes\rho_{loc}^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{V/\mathbb{D}}),

so that this coherent sheaf only depends on the integers
N,p,q
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.4.1
.

Using Lemma
2.4.3
, (i), and the fact that
\pi
is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{D}^{\prime\ast}
, the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho\circ\pi}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}}

is a semistable reduction diagram. Therefore, according to the definition of elementary exponents, for every pair of integers
(p,q)
, the elementary exponents
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{j}
can be described in terms of the cokernel of the injective map
u^{p,q}
from Lemma
2.4.4
. By this Lemma, this cokernel is isomorphic to the coherent sheaf:

\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}R^{q}g^{\prime}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}_{P}\otimes\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}}).

Using Lemma
2.4.3
, (iii), for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{P}
on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}
is annihilated by the function
g^{\ast}t^{\prime}
, hence can be written
i_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}\ast}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{P}
, where
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{P}
is some coherent sheaf on
\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}
, and
i_{\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}}
is the inclusion map.

This implies that the cokernel of
u^{p,q}
is isomorphic to the coherent sheaf:

\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}H^{q}(\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}})_{0},

where
(.)_{0}
denotes the skyscraper sheaf on
0
in
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
associated with a vector space.

We can rewrite this coherent sheaf as:

\bigoplus_{P\in\Sigma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}/(t^{\prime}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}}))^{\oplus h^{q}(\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}})}.

Hence by definition of the elementary exponents
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{j}
, there are precisely

\sum_{P\in\Sigma}h^{q}(\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}})

of them that are nonzero, and all of these are equal to
1/2
.

Using Lemma
2.4.5
, the number:

h^{q}(\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{P}\otimes(\rho^{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/\mathbb{D}})_{|\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{0}})

only depends on the integers
N,p,q,
and we shall denote it
c_{N}^{p,q}
. Consequently, the number of nonzero elementary exponents is
|\Sigma|.c_{N}^{p,q}
.

If there is only one critical point in the fiber,
3
3

3

An instance of this situation actually exists for every choice of
N\geq 1
. Consider for instance the hypersurface
X_{1}^{2}+\dots+X_{N}^{2}=tX_{0}^{2}
in
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{D}
.
the argument in
[
EFiMM21
]
shows that the number of nonzero elementary exponents is
0
unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=(N-1)/2
, in which case it is
1
. Consequently, the integer
c_{N}^{p,q}
is
0
unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=(N-1)/2
, in which case it is
1
, and we obtain in general that all the elementary exponents vanish unless
N-1
is even and
p=q=(N-1)/2
, in which case precisely
|\Sigma|
of them do not vanish, and they are equal to
1/2
.
∎

2.5.
Poincaré duality and Griffiths line bundles of cohomology in complementary degrees

We return to the notation of Section
2.3
. Namely, let
Y
be a smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N
,
C
be a connected smooth projective curve, and

g:Y\longrightarrow C

be a surjective morphism that is smooth over
{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=C-\Delta
, where
\Delta
is a finite subset of
C
, and such that the divisor
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings. Finally we denote:

Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=g^{-1}({\mathaccent 23{C}})=Y-Y_{\Delta}.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
C
.

Let
n
be an integer such that
0\leq n\leq 2(N-1)
.

For every point
x
in
\Delta
, let
(\alpha^{n}_{j,x})_{j}
be the union over the pairs
(p,q)
such that
p+q=n
, of the elementary exponents
(\alpha^{p,q}_{j,x})_{j,p,q}
of the
(p,q)
-Hodge bundle of the degeneration
g
at the point
x
. Let
\alpha^{n}_{x}
be their sum, and let us define a divisor in
C
with rational coefficients by:

A^{n}:=\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha^{n}_{x}\{x\}.

Observe that with the notation of the previous sections, this divisor is also given by:

A^{n}=\sum_{p,q\geq 0,p+q=n}A^{p,q}.

Let
r
be the least common multiple of the denominators of the rational numbers
(\alpha^{n}_{x})_{x\in\Delta}
, so that
rA^{n}
is a divisor with integral coefficients.

Lemma 2.5.1
.

With the above notation, the line bundle on
C
:

\big(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-}\big)^{\otimes r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(rA^{n})

is of 2-torsion.

Proof.

Let us denote simply
{\mathcal{V}}
the complex analytic vector bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
. It has an integral structure, associated to Betti cohomology with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients, so its determinant line bundle is of
2
-torsion, and we have an isomorphism of line bundles on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
:

\psi:{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathaccent 23{C}}}\longrightarrow(\det{\mathcal{V}})^{\otimes 2},

that sends
1
to
(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2}
, where
(v_{1},...,v_{s})
is any local integral frame of the vector bundle
{\mathcal{V}}
.

Let
x
be a point of
\Delta
, let
U
be a neighborhood of
x
with a local coordinate
t
centered in
x
, let
\infty
be a point in
U-\{x\}
, and let
v_{1},...,v_{s}
be an integral frame of the vector space
{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty}
.

Let
T
be the (counterclockwise) monodromy automorphism on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty}
around the point
x
. As the divisor
Y_{x}
in
Y
is a divisor with strict normal crossings, using Proposition
2.1.2
, this automorphism is quasi-unipotent.

As in the construction of the lower Deligne extension (see
[
Del70
, II, Prop. 5.4]
,
[
Kat76
, “Key Lemma” p. 547]
, and
[
EFiMM21
, section 2.1]
), let
B_{-}
be the only automorphism on
{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty}
whose eigenvalues have their real parts in
]-1,0]
, and satisfying the equality,

e^{2i\pi B_{-}}=T.

As the automorphism
T
is quasi-unipotent, these eigenvalues are rational numbers.

By Proposition
2.2.2
, the elementary exponents
(\alpha_{j,x}^{n})_{j}
are all in
[0,1[
, and by Proposition
2.2.3
, they satisfy that the
(e^{-2i\pi\alpha^{n}_{j,x}})_{j}
are the eigenvalues of the monodromy
T
.

Consequently, the eigenvalues of the automorphism
B_{-}
are exactly the
(-\alpha^{n}_{j,x})_{j}
.

By definition of the lower Deligne extension, we have an isomorphism of vector bundles on
U
:

\chi:{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\longrightarrow(\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{-})_{|U},

such that on every point
x^{\prime}
in
U
with coordinate
t(x^{\prime})
, an integral frame of
{\mathcal{V}}_{x^{\prime}}
is given by:

(\chi_{x^{\prime}}(e^{B_{-}(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))}.v_{j}))_{j},

depending on a choice of logarithm of
t(x^{\prime})
and of
t(\infty)
.

Observe that adding a multiple of
2i\pi
to
\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty))
multiplies the basis by
e^{2i\pi B_{-}}=T
, which respects the integral structure.

Let
x^{\prime}
be a point in
U
with coordinate
t(x^{\prime})
, and let us choose a logarithm
\log(t(x^{\prime}))
and a logarithm
\log(t(\infty))
. By definition of the morphisms
\psi
and
\chi
, the image of
1
by the morphism of vector spaces

(\det\chi_{x^{\prime}}^{-1})^{\otimes 2r}\circ\psi_{x^{\prime}}^{\otimes r}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow(\det{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty})^{\otimes 2r},

is given by:

\displaystyle((\det\chi_{x^{\prime}}^{-1})^{\otimes 2r}\circ\psi_{x^{\prime}}^{\otimes r})(1)

\displaystyle=(e^{B_{-}(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))}v_{1}\wedge...\wedge e^{B_{-}(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))}v_{s})^{2r}

\displaystyle=\det(e^{B_{-}(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))})^{2r}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}

\displaystyle=e^{2r\mathrm{tr}(B_{-}(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty))))}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}

\displaystyle=e^{2r(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))\mathrm{tr}(B_{-})}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}

(2.5.1)

\displaystyle=e^{2r(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))\sum_{j}(-\alpha^{n}_{j,x})}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}

\displaystyle=e^{-2r(\log(t(x^{\prime}))-\log(t(\infty)))\alpha^{n}_{x}}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}

(2.5.2)

\displaystyle=(t(x^{\prime})/t(\infty))^{-2r\alpha^{n}_{x}}.(v_{1}\wedge...\wedge v_{s})^{2r}.

The equality (
2.5.1
) holds because
the eigenvalues of the automorphism
B_{-}
are exactly the
(-\alpha^{n}_{j,x})_{j}
; (
2.5.2
) holds because
r\alpha^{n}_{x}
is an integer.
Observe that this expression does not depend on the choices of the logarithms
\log(t(x^{\prime}))
and
\log(t(\infty))
.

Consequently, the section
((\det\chi^{-1})^{\otimes 2r}\circ\psi_{|U-\{x\}}^{\otimes r})(1)
over
U-\{x\}
of the line bundle

(\det{\mathcal{V}}_{\infty})^{\otimes 2r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{U}

admits a pole of order
2r\alpha^{n}_{x}
in
x
.

Since
\chi
is an isomorphism of vector bundles, the section
\psi_{|U-\{x\}}^{\otimes r}(1)
of the line bundle

(\det{\mathcal{V}}_{|U-\{x\}})^{\otimes 2r}\simeq(\det\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{-})_{|U-\{x\}}^{\otimes 2r}

also admits a pole of order
2r\alpha^{n}_{x}
in
x
.

Consequently, the isomorphism of line bundles
\psi^{\otimes r}
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
can be extended into an isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\overline{\psi^{\otimes r}}_{-}:{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-2r\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha^{n}_{x}\{x\})={\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-2rA^{n})\longrightarrow(\det\overline{{\mathcal{V}}}_{-})^{\otimes 2r},

so that the line bundle on
C
:

\big(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-}\big)^{\otimes r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(rA^{n})

is of
2
-torsion, as wanted.
∎

Now, let us show the main result of this section.

Proposition 2.5.2
.

With the same notation as in Lemma
2.5.1
, the line bundle on
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\otimes-r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-r(N-1)A^{n})

is of
2
-torsion.

Moreover, if the local monodromy at every point of
\Delta
of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is unipotent, then so is the local monodromy of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
, and the line bundle on
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\vee}

is of
2
-torsion.

Proof.

Let us denote by
F^{\bullet}{\mathcal{H}}^{n}
(resp.
F^{\bullet}{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}
) the Hodge filtration by locally direct summands on the vector bundle
{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
(resp.
{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
).

Using Poincaré and Serre duality, we have an isomorphism of vector bundles on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
:

\varphi_{n}:{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})^{\vee},

that sends, for every integer
p
, the subbundle
F^{p}{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}
into the orthogonal subbundle:

(F^{N-p}{\mathcal{H}}^{n})^{\perp}\subset{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})^{\vee}.

Consequently, for every integer
p
, this isomorphism induces by restriction and quotient an isomorphism:

\varphi_{n}^{p}:\mathrm{gr}_{F}^{p}{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}\longrightarrow(F^{N-p}{\mathcal{H}}^{n})^{\perp}/(F^{N-1-p}{\mathcal{H}}^{n})^{\perp}\simeq(\mathrm{gr}_{F}^{N-1-p}{\mathcal{H}}^{n})^{\vee}.

Recall that the duality of vector bundles exchanges the upper and lower Deligne extensions, therefore, the isomorphism
\varphi_{n}
can be extended into an isomorphism of vector bundles on
C
:

\overline{\varphi_{n}}:\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{+}\longrightarrow(\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-})^{\vee},

that sends for every integer
p
, the subbundle
\overline{F^{p}{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{+}
into the
subbundle
(\overline{F^{N-p}{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})^{\perp}
, hence induces an isomorphism by restriction and quotient:

\overline{\varphi_{n}^{p}}:\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{+}}^{p}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{+}\longrightarrow(\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{-}}^{N-1-p}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})^{\vee}.

By definition, the upper Griffiths line bundle on
C
of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}(\det\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{+}}^{p}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{2(N-1)-n}}_{+})^{\otimes p}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigotimes_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}(\det\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{-}}^{N-1-p}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})^{\otimes-p}\textrm{ using the isomorphisms $(\overline{\varphi_{n}^{p}})_{p}$}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigotimes_{p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}(\det\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{-}}^{p^{\prime}}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})^{\otimes p^{\prime}-(N-1)}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigotimes_{p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}(\det\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{-}}^{p^{\prime}}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})^{\otimes p^{\prime}}\otimes\big(\bigotimes_{p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}(\det\mathrm{gr}_{\overline{F}_{-}}^{p^{\prime}}\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}_{-})\big)^{\otimes-(N-1)}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-})^{\otimes-(N-1)}.

Consequently, there is an isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\vee}\simeq\big(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-}\big)^{\otimes-(N-1)}.

Using Lemma
2.5.1
, the line bundle on
C
:

\big(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-}\big)^{\otimes r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(rA^{n})

is of
2
-torsion. Consequently, the line bundle on
C
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))^{\otimes(-r)}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-r(N-1)A^{n})

\displaystyle\simeq(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-})^{\otimes-r(N-1)}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-r(N-1)A^{n}),

\displaystyle\simeq\big[(\det\overline{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})}_{-})^{\otimes r}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(rA^{n})\big]^{\otimes-(N-1)},

is of
2
-torsion, as wanted.

For proving the second assertion in Proposition
2.5.2
, we use that the isomorphism
\varphi_{n}
is also compatible with the connections of both VHS, hence with their local monodromy. If the local monodromy at every point of
\Delta
of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
is unipotent, so is the local monodromy of the dual VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})^{\vee}
, and so is the monodromy of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y_{\mathaccent 23{C}}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
.

Finally, applying the first part of the proof with
A^{n}=0
and
r=1
, we obtain the last assertion in Proposition
2.5.2
.
∎

Chapter 3
Characteristic classes of relative differentials and of logarithmic relative differentials

In this chapter, we establish various results relating the Chern classes of sheaves of differentials, and vector bundles of logarithmic differentials. These results will be used in Chapter
4
to derive Theorems
1.3.2
and
4.2.3
from Theorems
1.3.1
and
4.2.1
, and are also of independent interest.

The statements of our results are gathered in Section
3.1
. The following sections, which contain their rather technical proofs and will not be referred to in the remainder of this paper, could be skipped at first reading.

3.1.
Comparing Chern classes and Todd classes of differentials and logarithmic differentials

3.1.1.
Chern classes of differentials and of logarithmic differentials: the absolute case
