Although we are ultimately interested in characteristic classes of sheaves of relative differentials, we shall first prove the following results in the absolute case.

Let
X
be a smooth
n
-dimensional complex scheme and let
E
be a reduced divisor with strict normal crossings in
X
. Let us denote by
(E_{i})_{i\in I}
the irreducible components of
E
, and for any subset
J\subseteq I
, let us define:
1
1

1

In particular,
E_{\emptyset}=X
.

E_{J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}E_{i}.

It is a smooth subscheme of codimension
|J|
in
X
, and we shall denote by:

i_{E_{J}}:E_{J}{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}X

the inclusion morphism.

As in
[
Del70
, II, 3.4]
, for every non-negative integer
r
, we denote by
E^{r}
the subscheme of codimension
r
of
X
defined as the union of the intersections of
r
distinct components of
E
:

E^{r}:=\bigcup_{J\subset I,|J|=r}E_{J}.

Observe that for every subset
J
in
I
, the subscheme:

E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1}=\bigcup_{i\in I-J}E_{J\cup\{i\}}

is a divisor with normal crossings in
E_{J}
.

Proposition 3.1.1
.

With the previous notation, for every subset
J
of
I
, the following relation holds between total Chern classes in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(E_{J})
:

(3.1.1)

c\big(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}\big)=c\big(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}(\log E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1})\big).

Moreover the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)
:

(3.1.2)

c(\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log E))=\sum_{J\subseteq I}i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

and the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(3.1.3)

{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))={\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{X})\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[E_{i}])^{-1},

where
{\rm{td}}(x)
denotes the formal series
x/(1-e^{-x})
.

The relation (
3.1.2
) may be reformulated as the following equalities, where
r
denotes an integer such that
0\leq r\leq n
:

(3.1.4)

c_{r}(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))=\sum_{J\subseteq I,|J|\leq r}i_{E_{J}\ast}c_{r-|J|}(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}).

3.1.2.
Notation

For the rest of the statements, let us adopt the following notation.

Let
Y
be a connected smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N
, let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve, and let

g:Y\longrightarrow C

be a surjective morphism that is smooth outside of
Y_{\Delta}
, where
\Delta
is a reduced divisor in
C
.

Let us assume that the divisor
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings, and let us write it as:

D=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}D_{i},

where
I
is a finite set and for every
i
in
I
,
m_{i}
is a positive integer, and where
D_{i}
is a smooth connected divisor such that the
(D_{i})_{i\in I}
intersect each other transversally.

As before, for every subset
J
of
I
, we denote:

D_{J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}D_{i},

and for every integer
r\geq 1
, we define the subscheme:

D^{r}:=\bigcup_{J\subset I,|J|=r}D_{J}.

The subscheme:

D_{J}\cap D^{|J|+1}=\bigcup_{i\in I-J}D_{J\cup\{i\}}

is a divisor with normal crossings in
D_{J}
.

For every element
i
in
I
, we denote by

{\mathcal{N}}_{i}:={\mathcal{N}}_{D_{i}}Y\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(D_{i})_{|D_{i}}

the normal vector bundle of the smooth divisor
D_{i}
in
Y
.

Let
\preceq
be a total order on
I
.

3.1.3.
Normal bundles to the strata
D_{J}
and Chern classes

Proposition 3.1.2
.

For every non-empty subset
J
in
I
, we have the equality of total Chern classes in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{J})
:

(3.1.5)

c\big(\omega^{1}_{Y/C|D_{J}}\big)=c\big(\Omega^{1}_{D_{J}}(\log D_{J}\cap D^{|J|+1})\big).

Proposition 3.1.3
.

Let
i
be an element in
I
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{i})
:

m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})=-\sum_{j\in I-\{i\}}m_{j}[D_{ij}],

where
D_{ij}:=D_{\{i,j\}}.

Corollary 3.1.4
.

Let
i
be an element in
I
and let
\beta
be a class in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{i})
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)
:

m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})\cap\beta)=-\sum_{j\in I-\{i\}}m_{j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}\beta_{|D_{ij}}.

Corollary 3.1.5
.

Let
(i,j)
be a pair in
I^{2}
such that
i\prec j
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{ij})
:

(3.1.6)

m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}})+m_{j}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}})=-\sum_{k\in I-\{i,j\}}m_{k}[D_{ijk}],

where
D_{ijk}:=D_{\{i,j,k\}}.

3.1.4.
Comparing the characteristic classes of
[T_{g}]
and
{\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C}}

Finally, we shall show the following results of comparison of the Chern classes of
\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
and of the relative tangent class in
K
-theory
2
2

2

In the notation of
[
SGA71
, Exposé VIII]
or
[
Ful98
, B.7.6]
, this class would be denoted by
T_{g}
.
:

[T_{g}]:=[T_{Y}]-g^{\ast}[T_{C}].

The duality operation on vector bundles on
Y
extends to an involution
.^{\vee}
of the abelian group
K^{0}(Y)
, which sends
[T_{g}]
to the class
[\Omega^{1}_{Y}]-g^{\ast}[\Omega^{1}_{C}]
.
Observe that we have a sequence of coherent sheaves on
Y
:

0\longrightarrow g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}\overset{Dg}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{Y}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y/C}\longrightarrow 0.

This sequence is exact: everything but the injectivity of the differential morphism
Dg
is a standard property of the sheaf of Kähler differentials, and since the morphism
g
is smooth on a dense open subset of
Y
, the morphism
Dg
does not vanish on a dense open subset, hence is injective.

Consequently, the following equality holds in
K^{0}(Y)
3
3

3

As discussed in
[
SGA71
, Exposé VIII, Section 2]
, the class
[T_{g}]
is the class in
K^{0}(Y)
of the relative tangent complex
\mathbb{T}_{g}
, defined as the dual of the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{g}
. The argument in the last paragraph actually proves that
\mathbb{L}_{g}
is quasi-isomorphic to the sheaf of Kähler differentials
\Omega^{1}_{g}=\Omega^{1}_{Y/C}
.
:

[T_{g}]=[\Omega^{1}_{Y/C}]^{\vee}.

Proposition 3.1.6
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(3.1.7)

\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}=\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])-1/2\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}],

where
{\rm{td}}(x)
denotes the formal series with rational coefficients
x/(1-e^{-x})
.

In particular, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(3.1.8)

\bigg[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\bigg]^{(1)}=-1/2\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)[D_{i}],

and the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(3.1.9)

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\bigg]^{(2)}

\displaystyle=1/12\sum_{i\in I}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})+1/4\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}[D_{ij}],

(3.1.10)

\displaystyle=1/12\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})[D_{ij}].

Proposition 3.1.7
.

For every non-negative integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

(3.1.11)

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}[c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}})]+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c_{r-|J|}(T_{D_{J}}).

Furthermore, if the subscheme
D^{3}
is empty, then for every non-negative integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

(3.1.12)

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}+m_{j}-1)i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}).

The remainder of this chapter shall be devoted to the proofs of these results.

More specifically, Section
3.2
shall be devoted to the proof of Proposition
3.1.1
; Section
3.3
shall be devoted to the proofs of Propositions
3.1.2
,
3.1.3
and Corollaries
3.1.4
and
3.1.5
; and Section
3.4
shall be devoted to the proofs of Propositions
3.1.6
and
3.1.7
.

3.2.
Characteristic classes of differentials and logarithmic differentials: the absolute case

Let us adopt the notation of Proposition
3.1.1
.

3.2.1.
Proof of (
3.1.1
)

Let
J
be a subset of
I
. Let

{\mathcal{N}}_{J}:={\mathcal{N}}_{E_{J}}X

be the normal vector bundle of the subscheme
E_{J}
in
X
.

One checks in local coordinates that the composition of morphisms of vector bundles on
E_{J}
:

{\mathcal{N}}_{J}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X|E_{J}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}

vanishes, so using the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}}_{J}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X|E_{J}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}}

and the exactness of the horizontal exact sequence of vector bundles, we obtain a map of vector bundles on
E_{J}
:

\alpha_{J}:\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}.

One checks in local coordinates that the map
\alpha_{J}
factors through a map of vector bundles:

\tilde{\alpha_{J}}:\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}(\log E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1})\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}.

On the other hand, let us define a map of vector bundles on
E_{J}
by:

\beta_{J}:=(\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}})_{i\in J}:\quad\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{J}}^{J}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{J}}^{\oplus|J|},

where, for every
i
in
J
,
\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}}
denotes the residue morphism relative to the divisor
E_{i}
.

Equality (
3.1.1
) follows immediately from the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

The morphisms
\tilde{\alpha_{J}}
and
\beta_{J}
fit together in an exact sequence of vector bundles on
E_{J}
:

0\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}(\log E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1})\overset{\tilde{\alpha_{J}}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}\overset{\beta_{J}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{J}}^{J}\longrightarrow 0.

Observe that this proposition follows from a log-structure argument (see
[
KS04
, Lemma 5.3.6]
). Indeed we may assume that
J
is non-empty and restrict ourselves to a formal neighborhood
U
of the divisor
E
in
X
, and consider the morphism of complex schemes:

g:U\longrightarrow S:={\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{C}[[t]]

defined by any equation of the divisor
E
in
U
. With the notation of
[
KS04
, 5.3]
, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
-module
\Omega^{1}_{U/S}(\log)
is precisely the quotient
\Omega^{1}_{U}(\log E)/g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{S}
.

Denoting by
s
the closed point in
S
, the morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{J}}
-modules

(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{S})_{\mid E_{J}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{U}(\log E)_{\mid E_{J}}

factors through the morphism

(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{S})_{\mid E_{J}}\longrightarrow(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{S}(\{s\}))_{\mid E_{J}}

which vanishes because
g
maps
E_{J}
to
s
, so that the canonical surjective morphism:

\Omega^{1}_{U}(\log E)_{\mid E_{J}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{U/S}(\log)_{\mid E_{J}}=\Omega^{1}_{U}(\log E)_{\mid E_{J}}/(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{S})_{\mid E_{J}}

is an isomorphism.

Under this isomorphism,
[
KS04
, Lemma 5.3.6, 2]
becomes Proposition
3.2.1
.

We present below a more direct proof for the convenience of the reader.

Proof.

We can check this on a neighborhood of a point
P
of
E_{J}
. Let
i_{1},...,i_{s}
be the elements of
J
, with
s:=|J|
.

Let
r
be the largest integer such that
P
is in
E^{r}
, in particular,
s\leq r\leq n
. Let
E_{i_{1}}
,…,
E_{i_{s}}
,
E_{i_{s+1}}
,…,
E_{i_{r}}
be the components of
E
that meet in
P
, with
\{i_{s+1},...,i_{r}\}
a subset of
I-J
.

Let us consider local coordinates
(x_{1},...,x_{n})
of
X
centered in
P
, such that for every integer
1\leq l\leq r
,
E_{i_{l}}
is locally defined by
(x_{l}=0)
.

The vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}
admits a local frame
(\mathrm{d}x_{s+1},...,\mathrm{d}x_{n})
, the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X|E_{J}}
admits a local frame (
\mathrm{d}x_{1}
,…,
\mathrm{d}x_{n}
), the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}
admits a local frame (
\mathrm{d}x_{1}/x_{1}
,…,
\mathrm{d}x_{r}/x_{r}
,
\mathrm{d}x_{r+1}
,…,
\mathrm{d}x_{n}
), and the canonical map from
\Omega^{1}_{X|E_{J}}
to
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}
sends, for every integer
l
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
to
x_{l}(\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l})
if
l\leq r
, else to
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
.

Consequently, the map

\alpha_{J}:\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}

sends, for every integer
l\geq s+1
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
to
x_{l}(\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l})
if
l\leq r
, else to
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
.

The divisor
E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1}
in
E_{J}
is locally defined by the equation
(x_{s+1}...x_{r}=0)
, so the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}(\log E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1})
admits a local frame
(\mathrm{d}x_{s+1}/x_{s+1},...,\mathrm{d}x_{r}/x_{r},\mathrm{d}x_{r+1},...,\mathrm{d}x_{n})
.

Consequently, the map

\tilde{\alpha_{J}}:\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}(\log E_{J}\cap E^{|J|+1})\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}

sends, for every integer
s+1\leq l\leq r
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l}
to itself, and for every integer
l>r
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
to itself. It is an injective morphism of vector bundles, whose image is the vector subbundle of
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}
generated by
(\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l})_{s+1\leq l\leq r}
and by
(\mathrm{d}x_{l})_{l>r}
.

On the other hand, the morphism of vector bundles:

\beta_{J}:\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{J}}^{J}

sends, for every integer
l
such that
1\leq l\leq s
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l}
to
(0,...,1,...,0)
with the
1
in
i_{l}
-th position; for every integer
l
such that
s+1\leq l\leq r
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l}
to
0
, and for every integer
l
such that
l>r
,
\mathrm{d}x_{l}
to
0
.

Consequently, it is surjective, and its kernel is the vector subbundle of
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{J}}
generated by
(\mathrm{d}x_{l}/x_{l})_{s+1\leq l\leq r}
and by
(\mathrm{d}x_{l})_{l>r}
, which is exactly the image of
\tilde{\alpha_{J}}
.

So the sequence is exact, as wanted.
∎

3.2.2.
Proof of (
3.1.2
)

We shall reason by induction over the dimension
n
of
X
.

If
n=0
, then
X
is a finite scheme and the divisor
E
is empty, so that equality (
3.1.2
) holds.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer, let us assume that equality (
3.1.2
) holds for schemes of dimension at most
n-1
, and let us prove it for a scheme
X
of dimension
n
.

One easily checks in local coordinates that we have an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
X
:

(3.2.1)

0\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X}\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)\overset{(\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}})_{i\in I}}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{i\in I}i_{E_{i}\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}\longrightarrow 0,

where
u
is the canonical inclusion morphism, and for every
i
in
I
,

\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}}
is the residue morphism.

Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)
:

(3.2.2)

c(\Omega^{1}_{X})=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))\prod_{i\in I}c([i_{E_{i}\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}])^{-1}.

For every element
i
in
I
, we have an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
X
:

(3.2.3)

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-E_{i})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\longrightarrow i_{E_{i}\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}\longrightarrow 0,

hence the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)
:

c([i_{E_{i}\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}])^{-1}=c({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-E_{i}))=1-[E_{i}].

Hence replacing in (
3.2.2
):

\displaystyle c(\Omega^{1}_{X})

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))\prod_{i\in I}(1-[E_{i}]),

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))\sum_{K\subset I}\prod_{i\in K}(-[E_{i}]),

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))\sum_{K\subset I}(-1)^{|K|}[E_{K}]\textrm{ because the $(E_{i})_{i\in I}$ intersect each other transversally,}

\displaystyle=\sum_{K\subset I}(-1)^{|K|}i_{E_{K}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)_{|E_{K}}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{K\subset I}(-1)^{|K|}i_{E_{K}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{K}}(\log E_{K}\cap E^{|K|+1}))\textrm{ using equality \eqref{eq Omega (log) _E_J},}

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))+\sum_{\emptyset\neq K\subset I}(-1)^{|K|}i_{E_{K}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{K}}(\log E_{K}\cap E^{|K|+1})).

Let
K
be a non-empty subset of
I
. Applying the induction hypothesis to the scheme
E_{K}
of dimension at most
n-1
and to the divisor with strict normal crossings
E_{K}\cap E^{|K|+1}
, whose components are the
(E_{K\cup\{i\}})_{i\in I-K}
, yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(E_{K})
:

c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{K}}(\log E_{K}\cap E^{|K|+1}))=\sum_{K\subset J\subset I}i_{E_{J},E_{K}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}).

Hence replacing in (
3.2.4
):

\displaystyle c(\Omega^{1}_{X})

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))+\sum_{\emptyset\neq K\subset J\subset I}(-1)^{|K|}i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}\Big[\sum_{\emptyset\neq K\subset J}(-1)^{|K|}\Big]i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}\Big[\prod_{i\in J}(1-1)-1\Big]i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))-\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

hence we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)
:

\displaystyle c(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))

\displaystyle=c(\Omega^{1}_{X})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\subset I}i_{E_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}}),

which concludes the induction and the proof of equality (
3.1.2
).

3.2.3.
Proof of (
3.1.3
)

Similarly to the proof of (
3.1.2
), using
exact sequences (
3.2.1
), (
3.2.3
) and the multiplicativity of the Todd class
{\rm{Td}}
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E))={\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{X})\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{Td}}([i_{E_{i}\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}])={\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{X})\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[E_{i}])^{-1},

as wanted.

3.3.
Normal bundles to the strata of the singular fibers and Chern classes

3.3.1.
Proof of Proposition
3.1.2

Let
J
be a non-empty subset of
I
. Since the line bundle
\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta)_{|\Delta}
can be trivialised on
\Delta
, the line bundle
(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta))_{|D_{J}}
can be trivialised on
D_{J}
, and we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{J})
:

c(g^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta))_{|D_{J}}=1,

hence by definition of the relative logarithmic vector bundle
\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
on
Y
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{J})
:

c(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})_{|D_{J}}=c(\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log Y_{\Delta}))_{|D_{J}},

hence applying equality (
3.1.1
) from Proposition
3.1.1
to the scheme
X:=Y
and the reduced divisor with strict normal crossings
E:=|D|=\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}
, and to the subset
J
in
I
:

c(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})_{|D_{J}}=c(\Omega^{1}_{D_{J}}(\log D_{J}\cap D^{|J|+1})),

as wanted.

3.3.2.
Proofs of Proposition
3.1.3
and Corollaries
3.1.4
and
3.1.5

Let
i
be an element of
I
. Since
\Delta
is a scheme of dimension
0
, the line bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{\Delta}C
on
\Delta
can be trivialized. Consequently, we can trivialize the following line bundle on
D_{i}
:

\displaystyle(g^{\ast}{\mathcal{N}}_{\Delta}C)_{|D_{i}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(Y_{\Delta})_{|D_{i}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(m_{i}D_{i})_{|D_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{j\in I-\{i\}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(m_{j}D_{j})_{|D_{i}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{i}^{\otimes m_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{j\in I-\{i\}}{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}(m_{j}D_{ij}).

Taking Chern classes, we obtain the following equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{i})
:

(3.3.1)

m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})=-\sum_{j\in I-\{i\}}m_{j}[D_{ij}],

which shows Proposition
3.1.3
.

If
\beta
is a class in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{i})
, intersecting (
3.3.1
) with
\beta
then pushing forward by the inclusion
i_{D_{i}}
yields Corollary
3.1.4
.

Now, let
(i,j)
be a pair in
I^{2}
such that
i\prec j
. Applying equality (
3.3.1
) with the element
i
then restricting to
D_{ij}
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{ij})
:

m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}})=-\sum_{k\in I-\{i\}}m_{k}[D_{ik}]_{|D_{ij}},

where
[D_{ik}]
is seen as a divisor in
D_{i}
.

If
k\neq j
, this divisor intersects transversally the divisor
D_{ij}
, so that we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{ij})
:

[D_{ik}]_{|D_{ij}}=[D_{ik}\cap D_{ij}]=[D_{ijk}].

If
k=j
, the self-intersection of the divisor
D_{ik}
is given by:

[D_{ik}]_{|D_{ij}}=c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{i})=c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}}).

We have used the canonical isomorphism of line bundles on
D_{ij}
:

{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{i}\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}}.

Hence replacing, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{ij})
:

m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}})=-m_{j}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}})-\sum_{k\in I-\{i,j\}}m_{k}[D_{ijk}],

which shows Corollary
3.1.5
.

3.4.
Characteristic classes of
[T_{g}]
and
\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C}

3.4.1.
Proof of Proposition
3.1.6

Using equality (
3.1.3
) from Proposition
3.1.1
applied to the scheme
X:=Y
and the reduced divisor with strict normal crossings
E:=|D|=\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log D))={\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y})\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[D_{i}])^{-1},

hence the equality:

{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y})={\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log D))\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[D_{i}]),

hence, using the definition of the relative logarithmic bundle
\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
and the multiplicativity of
{\rm{Td}}
:

{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y})={\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})\,g^{\ast}{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta))\,\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[D_{i}]).

Since
C
is a curve, we have a canonical isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta)\simeq\Omega^{1}_{C}(\Delta),

hence replacing:

\displaystyle{\rm{Td}}(\Omega^{1}_{Y})

\displaystyle={\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})\,g^{\ast}{\rm{td}}(c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{C})+[\Delta])\,\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}(-[D_{i}]).

Taking duals, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(3.4.1)

{\rm{Td}}(T_{Y})={\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\,g^{\ast}{\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])\,\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}]).

By definition of the relative tangent class
[T_{g}]
in
K^{0}(Y)
, we have the identity in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])

\displaystyle={\rm{Td}}(T_{Y})g^{\ast}{\rm{Td}}(T_{C})^{-1},

\displaystyle={\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\,g^{\ast}[{\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta]){\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C}))^{-1}]\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])\textrm{ using \eqref{first equality td omega},}

hence the equality:

(3.4.2)

\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}=g^{\ast}[{\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta]){\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C}))^{-1}]\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}]).

Since
C
is a curve, all the classes in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
vanish. Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle{\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta]){\rm{td}}(c_{1}(T_{C}))^{-1}

\displaystyle=[1+1/2\,(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])][1+1/2\,c_{1}(T_{C})]^{-1},

\displaystyle=[1+1/2(c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])][1-1/2\ c_{1}(T_{C})],

\displaystyle=1-1/2\,[\Delta].

Hence replacing in (
3.4.2
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}

\displaystyle=g^{\ast}[1-1/2\,[\Delta]]\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}]),

(3.4.3)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])-1/2\,(g^{\ast}[\Delta])\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}]).

The line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(\Delta)_{|\Delta}
on
\Delta
can be trivialized, so for every element
i
in
I
, the line bundle
(g^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(\Delta))_{|D_{i}}
on
D_{i}
can be trivialized. Consequently, for every element
i
in
I
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)
:

(g^{\ast}[\Delta])[D_{i}]=0,

hence we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(g^{\ast}[\Delta])\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])=g^{\ast}[\Delta]=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}].

Hence replacing in (
3.4.3
), we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}=\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])-1/2\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}],

which shows equality (
3.1.7
).

Taking the terms of codimension 1, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big]^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])^{(1)}-1/2\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}],

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}(1/2[D_{i}])-1/2\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}],

\displaystyle=-1/2\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)[D_{i}],

which shows equality (
3.1.8
).

Taking instead the terms of codimension 2, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big]^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\big[\prod_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])\big]^{(2)},

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])^{(2)}+\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}{\rm{td}}([D_{i}])^{(1)}.{\rm{td}}([D_{j}])^{(1)},

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}(1/12\,[D_{i}]^{2})+\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(1/2\,[D_{i}]).(1/2[D_{j}]),

(3.4.4)

\displaystyle=1/12\,\sum_{i\in I}[D_{i}]^{2}+1/4\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}[D_{i}].[D_{j}].

For every element
i
in
I
, the self-intersection of the divisor
D_{i}
is given in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)
by:

[D_{i}]^{2}=i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i}).

On the other hand, for every pair
(i,j)
in
I^{2}
such that
i\prec j
, the divisors
D_{i}
and
D_{j}
intersect transversally, so that we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)
:

[D_{i}].[D_{j}]=[D_{ij}].

Hence replacing in (
3.4.4
):

\Big[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big]^{(2)}=1/12\sum_{i\in I}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})+1/4\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}[D_{ij}],

which shows equality (
3.1.9
).

Finally, for every element
i
in
I
, applying Proposition
3.1.3
and dividing by
m_{i}
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(D_{i})_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})=-\sum_{j\in I-\{i\}}m_{j}/m_{i}[D_{ij}],

hence pushing forward by the inclusion of
D_{i}
in
Y
, then summing over
i
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{i\in I}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})=-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}/m_{j}+m_{j}/m_{i})[D_{ij}],

hence replacing, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big]^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-1/12\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}/m_{j}+m_{j}/m_{i})[D_{ij}]+1/4\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}[D_{ij}],

\displaystyle=1/12\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})[D_{ij}],

which shows equality (
3.1.10
).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
3.1.6
.

3.4.2.
Proof of Proposition
3.1.7

Using equality (
3.1.2
) from Proposition
3.1.1
applied to the scheme
X:=Y
and the reduced divisor with strict normal crossings
E:=|D|=\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)
:

c(\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log D))=c(\Omega^{1}_{Y})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{D_{J}}),

hence, by definition of the relative logarithmic bundle
\omega^{1}_{Y/C}
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)
:

\displaystyle c(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})

\displaystyle=\big[c(\Omega^{1}_{Y})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{D_{J}})\big]\,g^{\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta))^{-1}.

Since
C
is a curve, we have a canonical isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\Omega^{1}_{C}(\log\Delta)\simeq\Omega^{1}_{C}(\Delta),

hence replacing:

c(\omega^{1}_{Y/C})=\big[c(\Omega^{1}_{Y})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{D_{J}})\big]\,g^{\ast}(1+c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{C})+[\Delta])^{-1},

hence taking duals:

(3.4.5)

c(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=\big[c(T_{Y})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}})\big]\,g^{\ast}(1+c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])^{-1}.

The line bundles
T_{C|\Delta}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(\Delta)_{|\Delta}
on
\Delta
can be trivialized, so for every non-empty subset
J
in
I
, the line bundles
(g^{\ast}T_{C})_{|D_{J}}
and
(g^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(\Delta))_{|D_{J}}
on
D_{J}
can be trivialized. Consequently, for every non-empty subset
J
in
I
, we have the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)
:

[i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}})](g^{\ast}c_{1}(T_{C}))=[i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}})](g^{\ast}[\Delta])=0,

hence we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Y)
:

\bigg[\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}})\bigg]\,g^{\ast}(1+c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])^{-1}=\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}}).

Hence replacing in (
3.4.5
), we obtain the equality:

(3.4.6)

c(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=c(T_{Y})g^{\ast}(1+c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])^{-1}+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}}).

Since
C
is a curve, all the classes in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(C)
vanish. Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(C)
:

(1+c_{1}(T_{C})-[\Delta])^{-1}=(1+c_{1}(T_{C}))^{-1}+[\Delta].

Hence replacing in (
3.4.6
):

\displaystyle c(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})

\displaystyle=c(T_{Y})g^{\ast}[(1+c_{1}(T_{C}))^{-1}+[\Delta]]+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}}),

\displaystyle=c(T_{Y})g^{\ast}c(T_{C})^{-1}+c(T_{Y})\big(\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}[D_{i}]\big)+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}}),

\displaystyle=c([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}c(T_{Y|D_{i}})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c(T_{D_{J}})\textrm{ by definition of the class $[T_{g}]$.}

Let
r
be a non-negative integer, taking the terms of codimension
r
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

(3.4.7)

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{Y|D_{i}})+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c_{r-|J|}(T_{D_{J}}).

For every element
i
in
I
, we have an exact sequence of vector bundles on
D_{i}
:

0\longrightarrow T_{D_{i}}\longrightarrow T_{Y|D_{i}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\longrightarrow 0,

hence the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(D_{i})
:

c(T_{Y|D_{i}})=c(T_{D_{i}})(1+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})),

hence the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r-1}(D_{i})
,

c_{r-1}(T_{Y|D_{i}})=c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}).

Hence replacing in (
3.4.7
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}[c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}})]+\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}i_{D_{J}\ast}c_{r-|J|}(T_{D_{J}}),

which shows equality (
3.1.11
).

Now, let us assume that the subscheme
D^{3}
is empty. In this case, equality (
3.1.11
) can be rewritten:

\displaystyle c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})

\displaystyle=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}[c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}})]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in I}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})+\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}),

\displaystyle=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})

(3.4.8)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}))+\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}).

For every element
i
in
I
, applying Corollary
3.1.4
to the class
\beta:=c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}})
in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r-2}(D_{i})
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

(3.4.9)

m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}))=-\sum_{j\in I-\{i\}}m_{j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}|D_{ij}}),

hence summing over
i
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}))=-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}(m_{j}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}|D_{ij}})+m_{i}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{j}|D_{ij}})).

For every pair
(i,j)
in
I^{2}
such that
i\prec j
, we have the following normal exact sequences of vector bundles on
D_{ij}
:

0\longrightarrow T_{D_{ij}}\longrightarrow T_{D_{i}|D_{ij}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{i}\longrightarrow 0,

0\longrightarrow T_{D_{ij}}\longrightarrow T_{D_{j}|D_{ij}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{j}\longrightarrow 0,

hence the following equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r-2}(D_{ij})
:

c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}|D_{ij}})=c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{i})c_{r-3}(T_{D_{ij}}),

c_{r-2}(T_{D_{j}|D_{ij}})=c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{j})c_{r-3}(T_{D_{ij}}),

hence using the canonical isomorphisms of line bundles on
D_{ij}
between
{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{j}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}}
, as well as between
{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{ij}}D_{i}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}}
:

c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}|D_{ij}})=c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}})c_{r-3}(T_{D_{ij}}),

c_{r-2}(T_{D_{j}|D_{ij}})=c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})+c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}})c_{r-3}(T_{D_{ij}}),

hence replacing in (
3.4.9
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)
:

\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}))=-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{j}+m_{i})i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})\\
-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}[\{m_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i|D_{ij}})+m_{j}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{j|D_{ij}})\}c_{r-3}(T_{D_{ij}}];

using Corollary
3.1.5
, and using that
D^{3}
is empty, we get:

\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}i_{D_{i}\ast}(c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{r-2}(T_{D_{i}}))=-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}+m_{j})i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}).

Hence replacing in (
3.4.8
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})

\displaystyle=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})

\displaystyle-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}+m_{j})i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}})+\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}),

\displaystyle=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{D_{i}})-\sum_{(i,j)\in I^{2},i\prec j}(m_{i}+m_{j}-1)i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{D_{ij}}).

This proves equality (
3.1.12
).

Chapter 4
The alternating product of Griffiths line bundles associated to a pencil of complete varieties with SNC degenerations

In this chapter, we work over a base field
k
of characteristic zero, and by the word “scheme”, we mean a separated algebraic scheme of finite type over
k
.

4.1.
The characteristic classes
\rho
and
\rho_{r}
and the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formula

In this section, we will work on a fixed smooth
k
-scheme
X
.

4.1.1.
The classes
\lambda_{y}
,
\varphi_{y}
, and
\rho

Recall that the
Grothendieck
\lambda
-map
:

\lambda_{y}:K^{0}(X)\longrightarrow 1+y\,K^{0}(X)[[y]],

where
y
denotes an indeterminate,
is defined as the only map that is multiplicative for exact sequences, and that is given on (the
K
-theory class of) a vector bundle
V
by:

\lambda_{y}([V]):=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{{\rm rk}(V)}y^{i}\left[\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{i}V\right]\in 1+y\,K^{0}(X)[y];

see for instance
[
SGA71
, app. to Exposé 0]
.

Definition 4.1.1
.

For every vector bundle
V
on
X
, we define
\varphi_{y}(V)
as the element of the polynomial ring
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[y]
given by:

(4.1.1)

\varphi_{y}(V):={\rm{ch}}\big(\lambda_{y}(V^{\vee})\big)\,\,{\rm{Td}}(V)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{{\rm rk}(V)}y^{i}{\rm{ch}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{i}V^{\vee}\right)\,{\rm{Td}}(V).

The ring
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[y]
has a new grading constructed in terms of the existing natural bigrading:

\left({\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[y]\right)^{(d)}:=\overset{d}{\underset{e=0}{\bigoplus}}{\mathrm{CH}}^{e}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}[y]_{d-e}.

For every vector bundle
V
over
X
, the term of total degree
0
of the element
\varphi_{y}(V)
is
1
, so this element is in
1+({\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[y])^{(\geq 1)}.

Proposition 4.1.2
.

The map
\varphi_{y}
is multiplicative for short exact sequences.

Proof.

The
\lambda
-map
\lambda_{y}
and the Todd class
{\rm{Td}}
are both multiplicative for short exact sequences, and the Chern character
{\rm{ch}}
is a morphism of rings.
∎

Remark 4.1.3
.

We could use this to descend
\varphi_{y}
to a morphism of groups from
K^{0}(X)
to
1+\left({\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[[y]]\right)^{(\geq 1)}
, but we would then lose the property that
\varphi_{y}(V)
is a polynomial, which is necessary for our purpose.

Proposition 4.1.4
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
, we have the identity in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}[y]
:

(4.1.2)

\varphi_{y}(L)={\rm{td}}(c_{1}(L))\,\left[1+y\,e^{-c_{1}(L)}\right].

In particular, replacing
y
with
-1
:

(4.1.3)

\varphi_{-1}(L)=c_{1}(L).

Recall that, in the right-hand side of (
4.1.2
), we denote by

{\rm{td}}(x)
the formal series
x/(1-e^{-x})
.

Proof.

The first equality (
4.1.2
) comes from a direct computation of
\varphi_{y}(L)
, using the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

{\rm{ch}}(L^{\vee})=e^{-c_{1}(L)}.

The second equality (
4.1.3
) follows from the definition of the series
{\rm{td}}
.
∎

The identity (
4.1.3
) is also a particular case of
[
Ful98
, Ex. 3.2.5]
. Indeed, the upcoming Proposition
4.1.8
can be seen as a variant of this example.

Definition 4.1.5
.

If
V
is a vector bundle on
X
, the class
\rho(V)
is defined by:

\rho(V):=\dot{\varphi}_{-1}(V)=\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}{\rm{ch}}(\lambda_{y}(V^{\vee}))\Big)_{|y=-1}{\rm{Td}}(V)\in{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}.

the derivative of
\varphi
applied to
y=-1
, it is well-defined since
\varphi_{y}(V)
is a polynomial.

If
r\geq 1
is an integer, we also define the characteristic class:

\rho_{r}:=c_{r-1}-\frac{r}{2}c_{r}+\frac{1}{12}c_{1}c_{r}:K^{0}(X)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}.

We are interested in comparing the classes
\rho(V)
and
\rho_{r}([V])
when
V
is a vector bundle of rank
r
.

Proposition 4.1.6
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
, we have the identity in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(4.1.4)

\rho(L)={\rm{td}}(-c_{1}(L))=:{\rm{Td}}(L^{\vee}).

Proof.

We differentiate the equality (
4.1.2
). Since
\varphi_{y}(L)
is a polynomial of degree at most 1, its derivative is its coefficient of degree
1
, which gives the equality:

\rho(L)={\rm{td}}(c_{1}(L))\,e^{-c_{1}(L)}.

Now, we have an equality of formal series in an indeterminate
x
:

{\rm{td}}(x)\,e^{-x}={\rm{td}}(-x),

that we can show using the definition of
{\rm{td}}
in terms of exponentials, as follows:

\displaystyle{\rm{td}}(x)\,e^{-x}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{x}{1-e^{-x}}\bigg)\,e^{-x},

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{e^{x}-1},

\displaystyle=-\frac{x}{1-e^{-(-x)}},

\displaystyle={\rm{td}}(-x).

Replacing
x
with
c_{1}(L)
, this becomes the result.
∎

Corollary 4.1.7
.

Let
V
be a vector bundle of rank
r
on
X
, and let

V=V_{r}\supset...\supset V_{0}=0

be a filtration such that
L_{i}:=V_{i}/V_{i-1}
is a line bundle for every integer
i\in\{1,\dots,r\}.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\rho(V)=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}{\rm{td}}(-c_{1}(L_{i}))\,\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j}).

Proof.

Using Proposition
4.1.2
, the map
\varphi_{y}
is multiplicative for exact sequences, so we have the equality:

\varphi_{y}(V)=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{r}\varphi_{y}(L_{i}).

Differentiating and applying the Leibniz rule, we obtain:

\dot{\varphi}_{-1}(V)=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\dot{\varphi}_{-1}(L_{i})\,\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}\varphi_{-1}(L_{j}).

Using (
4.1.3
) and (
4.1.4
), we obtain the result.
∎

This allows us to compare
\rho
and
\rho_{r}
:

Proposition 4.1.8

(compare
[
BCOV94
, p. 374]
)
.

Let
V
be a vector bundle of rank
r
on
X
, we have the identity in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(4.1.5)

\rho(V)=\rho_{r}([V])+(\textrm{terms of codimension}\geq r+2).

Proof.

First, suppose that
V
has a filtration by subbundles
V=V_{r}\supset...\supset V_{0}=0
, such that for
1\leq i\leq r
,
L_{i}:=V_{i}/V_{i-1}
is a line bundle.
Using Corollary
4.1.7
, we have the equality:

\rho(V)=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}{\rm{td}}(-c_{1}(L_{i}))\,\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j}).

Recall the first terms of the development of
{\rm{td}}
:

{\rm{td}}(x)=1+\frac{x}{2}+\frac{x^{2}}{12}+...\quad.

Accordingly we have:

\displaystyle\rho(V)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\Bigg[\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}(L_{i})}{2}+\frac{c_{1}(L_{i})^{2}}{12}+(\textrm{terms of codimension}\geq 3)\Big)\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})\Bigg],

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})-\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})+\frac{1}{12}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\Big(c_{1}(L_{i})\,\prod\limits_{j=1}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})\Big)+(\textrm{codim}\geq r+2)),

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})-\frac{r}{2}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})+\frac{1}{12}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}c_{1}(L_{i})\Big)\prod\limits_{j=1}^{r}c_{1}(L_{j})+(\textrm{codim}\geq r+2),

where by
(\textrm{codim}\geq r+2)
we mean an element in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\geq r+2}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

By multiplicativity of the total Chern class, the elementary symmetric polynomials of
(c_{1}(L_{i}))_{i}
are simply the Chern classes of
V
, which gives the equality:

\displaystyle\rho(V)

\displaystyle=c_{r-1}(V)-\frac{r}{2}c_{r}(V)+\frac{1}{12}c_{1}(V)c_{r}(V)+(\textrm{codim}\geq r+2),

\displaystyle=\rho_{r}([V])+(\textrm{codim}\geq r+2)).

This shows the desired formula when
V
has such a filtration.

In the general case, we are reduced to this situation by the “splitting principle” (
[
Ful98
, Remark 3.2.3]
).
∎

4.1.2.
The class
\rho
and the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formula

The Riemann-Roch formula implies the following result.

Proposition 4.1.9
.

Let
X,S
be two smooth
k
-schemes of pure dimension, let:

f:X\longrightarrow S

be a proper morphism of relative dimension
m\geq 0
, and let
V
be a vector bundle on
X
.
Let us denote by

[T_{f}]:=[T_{X/k}]-f^{\ast}[T_{S/k}]\in K^{0}(X)

the relative tangent class associated with the l.c.i. morphism
f
.
1
1

1

see for instance
[
SGA71
, Exposé VIII]
or
[
Ful98
, B.7.6]
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(S)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

f_{\ast}\left(\rho(V)\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{f}])}{{\rm{Td}}(V)}\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V),\\
0\leq q\leq m\end{subarray}}p(-1)^{p+q-1}{\rm{ch}}(R^{q}f_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee}).

In particular, taking the terms of codimension
1
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(S)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

f_{\ast}\left[\left(\rho(V)\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{f}])}{{\rm{Td}}(V)}\right)^{(m+1)}\right]=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V),\\
0\leq q\leq m\end{subarray}}p(-1)^{p+q-1}c_{1}(R^{q}f_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee}).

Proof.

We have the equality:

(4.1.6)

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\left(\rho(V)\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{f}])}{{\rm{Td}}(V)}\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\ast}\left(\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}{\rm{ch}}(\lambda_{y}(V^{\vee}))\Big)_{|y=-1}{\rm{Td}}(V)\frac{{\rm{Td}}[T_{f}]}{{\rm{Td}}(V)}\right),

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}f_{\ast}\left({\rm{ch}}(\lambda_{y}(V^{\vee})){\rm{Td}}([T_{f}])\right)_{|y=-1},

(4.1.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}f_{\ast}\left(\sum_{0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V)}y^{p}{\rm{ch}}(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee}){\rm{Td}}([T_{f}])\right)_{|y=-1},

(4.1.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}\left(\sum_{0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V)}y^{p}{\rm{ch}}(R^{\bullet}f_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee})\right)_{|y=-1},

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V)}p(-1)^{p-1}{\rm{ch}}(R^{\bullet}f_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee}),

(4.1.9)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq p\leq{\rm rk}(V)\\
0\leq q\leq m\end{subarray}}p(-1)^{p-1}(-1)^{q}{\rm{ch}}(R^{q}f_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}V^{\vee}),

where in (
4.1.6
), we have used the definition of the class
\rho
; in (
4.1.7
), we have used the definition of the class
\lambda_{y}
; in (
4.1.8
), we have used the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formula; and in (
4.1.9
), we have used the definition of the morphism of abelian groups
R^{\bullet}f_{\ast}
.
∎

4.2.
Application to Griffiths line bundles of fibrations over curves with SNC fibers

4.2.1.
An application of Steenbrink’s theory

We are now able to compute a certain combination of the Griffiths line bundles in terms of the function
\rho_{r}
.

Theorem 4.2.1
.

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
,
Y
be a connected smooth projective
N
-dimensional complex scheme, and let

g:Y\longrightarrow C

be a surjective morphism of complex schemes. Let us assume that there exists a finite subset
\Delta
in
C
such that
g
is smooth over
C-\Delta
, and such that the divisor
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings in
Y
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=g_{\ast}\left[\left(\rho_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\right)^{(N)}\right].

Proof.

Using Proposition
2.1.4
, for every integer
n
, we have an isomorphism of line bundles over
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\simeq\bigotimes_{0\leq p\leq n}\big(\det R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}\big)^{\otimes p}.

Consequently, taking Chern classes and taking the alternate sum, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{0\leq p\leq n}pc_{1}(R^{n-p}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}).

We reindex this sum on
p
and
q:=n-p
, and we get:

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,q\geq 0,\\
p+q\leq 2(N-1)\end{subarray}}p(-1)^{p+q-1}c_{1}(R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}).

Since the morphism
g
is of relative dimension
N-1
, the term in the sum vanishes unless
p,q\leq N-1
, so we can rewrite:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p,q\leq N-1}p(-1)^{p+q-1}c_{1}(R^{q}g_{\ast}\omega^{p}_{Y/C}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq p,q\leq N-1}p(-1)^{p+q-1}c_{1}(R^{q}g_{\ast}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\bigwedge}}{{\bigwedge}}{{\textstyle\wedge}}{{\scriptstyle\wedge}}^{p}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})^{\vee}).

Now, we apply Proposition
4.1.9
to the morphism
g
of relative dimension
N-1
and the locally free sheaf

V:=\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C}

of rank
N-1
. This yields the equality:

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=g_{\ast}\left[\left(\rho(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\right)^{(N)}\right],

hence applying Proposition
4.1.8
to the vector bundle
V
of rank
N-1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=g_{\ast}\left[\left(\{\rho_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})+(\textrm{terms of codimension}\geq N+1)\}\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\right)^{(N)}\right],

\displaystyle=g_{\ast}\left[\left(\rho_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\right)^{(N)}\right].\qed

4.2.2.
The alternating product of Griffiths line bundles and the geometry of singular fibers

We adopt the notation of the previous subsection, and assume, without loss of generality, that the divisor
\Delta
in
C
is reduced.

Let us adopt the notation of Subsection
3.1.2
. Namely, we write the divisor with strict normal crossings
Y_{\Delta}
as:

D=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}D_{i},

where
I
is a finite set, for every
i
in
I
,
m_{i}\geq 1
is an integer, and
D_{i}
is a smooth connected divisor, such that the
(D_{i})_{i\in I}
intersect each other transversally. We denote by
{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
the normal bundle
N_{D_{i}}Y
of
D_{i}
in
Y
.

The set
I
may be written as the disjoint union:

I=\bigcup_{x\in\Delta}I_{x},

where, for every
x\in\Delta,

I_{x}
denotes the non-empty subset of
I
defined by:

I_{x}:=\big\{i\in I\mid g(D_{i})=\{x\}\big\}.

For every subset
J
of
I
, let us denote:

D_{J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}D_{i},

it is a smooth subscheme of codimension
|J|
.

We adopt the notation of
[
Del70
, II, 3.4]
. Namely, we denote, for every integer
r\geq 1
,
D^{r}
the subscheme of codimension
r
of
Y
defined by the union of all the intersections of
r
different components
(D_{i})
:

D^{r}:=\bigcup_{J\subset I,|J|=r}D_{J}.

Let us choose a total order
\preceq
on
I
.

For every element
i
in
I
, let us define an open subset
{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}
of the divisor
D_{i}
by:

{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}:=D_{i}-D_{i}\cap D^{2}.

Similarly, for every pair
(i,j)
in
I^{2}
such that
i\prec j
, let us define an open subset
{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}
of the subscheme

D_{ij}:=D_{\{i,j\}}

by:

{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}:=D_{ij}-D_{ij}\cap D^{3}.

Let
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}
denote the topological Euler characteristic.

First recall the following classical theorem:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
X
be a proper smooth
n
-dimensional complex scheme and
E
be a reduced divisor with strict normal crossings in
X
. We have the equality of integers:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X-E):=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X-E,\mathbb{C})=\int_{X}c_{n}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{X}(\log E)).

Proof.

We can give a proof of this theorem using the results of this paper. Let us adopt the notation of Proposition
3.1.1
, we have the equality of integers:

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{n}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{X}(\log E))

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n}\int_{X}c_{n}(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log E)),

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n}\sum_{J\subset I}\int_{E_{J}}c_{n-|J|}(\Omega^{1}_{E_{J}})\textrm{ using equality \eqref{Chern class Omega1log} from Proposition \ref{comparison Omega log and Omega easy cases general},}

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}\int_{E_{J}}c_{n-|J|}(T_{E_{J}}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\subset I}(-1)^{|J|}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{J}),

\displaystyle=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)-\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E)\textrm{ using the inclusion-exclusion formula,}

\displaystyle=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X-E).\qed

Theorem 4.2.3
.

With the notation of this subsection, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(4.2.1)

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=\frac{1}{12}g_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})+\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha_{x}[x],

where for every
x
in
\Delta
,
\alpha_{x}
is the rational number given by:

(4.2.2)

\displaystyle\alpha_{x}=\frac{N-1}{4}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}(m_{i}-1)\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}),

(4.2.3)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}\Big[3(N-1)(m_{i}-1)\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})+\int_{D_{i}}c_{1}({\mathcal{N}}_{i})c_{N-2}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2}))\Big]+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}).

The expression (
4.2.2
) for
\alpha_{x}
involves only the topology of the open strata
{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i}
and
{\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}
of the singular fibers of
g
and the multiplicities
m_{i}
of their components
D_{i}
. The expression (
4.2.3
) makes clear that
\alpha_{x}
belongs to
(1/12)\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.2.1
and by definition of the class
\rho_{N-1}
, the following equalities hold in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}

\displaystyle(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=g_{\ast}\Big[\Big(\rho_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C}).\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(N)}\Big],

\displaystyle=g_{\ast}\Big[\Big((c_{N-2}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})-\frac{N-1}{2}c_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})+\frac{1}{12}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C}))\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(N)}\Big],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}g_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})-\frac{N-1}{2}g_{\ast}\Big[c_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\,\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(1)}\Big]

(4.2.4)

\displaystyle+g_{\ast}\Big[c_{N-2}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(2)}\Big].

Using successively equality (
3.1.8
) from Proposition
3.1.6
and Proposition
3.1.2
,
we get the following equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle c_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\,\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C|D_{i}}),

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2}))

hence pushing forward by
g
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle g_{\ast}\Big[c_{N-1}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\,\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(1)}\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I}(m_{i}-1)g_{\ast}i_{D_{i}\ast}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2})),

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha_{1,x}[x],

where for every point
x
in
\Delta
,
\alpha_{1,x}
is the rational number given by:

\displaystyle\alpha_{1,x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}(m_{i}-1)\int_{D_{i}}c_{N-1}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{i}}(\log D_{i}\cap D^{2})),

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}(m_{i}-1)\,\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})\textrm{ \quad\quad using Theorem \ref{chi_top complement of DNC 2}.}

Similarly, using equality (
3.1.10
) from Proposition
3.1.6
and Proposition
3.1.2
, the following equalities hold in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(Y)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle c_{N-2}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{N-2}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C|D_{ij}}),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})i_{D_{ij}\ast}c_{N-2}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{ij}}(\log D_{ij}\cap D^{3})),

hence pushing forward by
g
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

g_{\ast}\Big[c_{N-2}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})\Big(\frac{{\rm{Td}}([T_{g}])}{{\rm{Td}}(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})}\Big)^{(2)}\Big]=\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha_{2,x}[x],

where, for every point
x
in
\Delta
,
\alpha_{2,x}
is the rational number given by:

\displaystyle\alpha_{2,x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})\int_{D_{ij}}c_{N-2}(\Omega^{1\vee}_{D_{ij}}(\log D_{ij}\cap D^{3})),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij})\textrm{ using again Theorem \ref{chi_top complement of DNC 2}.}

Hence replacing in (
4.2.4
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=\frac{1}{12}g_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})+\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha_{x}[x],

where for every point
x
in
\Delta
, the rational number
\alpha_{x}
is given by:

\displaystyle\alpha_{x}=-\frac{N-1}{2}\alpha_{1,x}+\alpha_{2,x},

\displaystyle=\frac{N-1}{4}\sum_{i\in I_{x}}(m_{i}-1)\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{i})+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I_{x}^{2},\\
i\prec j\end{subarray}}(3-m_{i}/m_{j}-m_{j}/m_{i})\chi_{\mathrm{top}}({\mathaccent 23{D}}_{ij}).

For the other expression of the rational number
\alpha_{x}
, one reasons similarly using the expression from (
3.1.9
) from Proposition
3.1.6
instead of (
3.1.10
).
∎

Chapter 5
The alternating product of Griffiths line bundles associated to a proper fibration with non-degenerate critical points

The objective of this chapter is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 5.0.1
.

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
,
H
be a smooth projective
N
-dimensional complex scheme, and let

f:H\longrightarrow C

be a morphism of complex schemes. Let us assume that there exists a finite subset
\Sigma
in
H
such that
f
is smooth on
H-\Sigma
and admits a non-degenerate critical point at any point of
\Sigma
.

Let
[\Sigma]
be the reduced
0
-cycle in
H
associated with the finite subset
\Sigma
.

We have the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(5.0.1)

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\big)=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}\big((c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]^{\vee})\big)+u^{-}_{N}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

and

(5.0.2)

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\big)=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}\big((c_{1}c_{N-1}\big)([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]^{\vee})\big)+u_{N}^{+}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

where
u^{-}_{N}
and
u^{+}_{N}
are the rational numbers defined by:

u^{-}_{N}:=\begin{cases}(5N-3)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
N/24&\text{if $N$ is even},\end{cases}

and:

u^{+}_{N}:=\begin{cases}-(7N-9)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
N/24&\text{if $N$ is even}.\end{cases}

Observe that, as the morphism of non-singular complex schemes
f:H\rightarrow C
is smooth on an open dense subset of
H
, we have the following equality in
K^{0}(H)\simeq K_{0}(H)
:

[T_{f}]=[\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]^{\vee},

where
.^{\vee}
denotes the duality involution on
K^{0}(H)
, by the same reasoning as in
Subsection
3.1.4
.

5.1.
Reduction of the proof of Theorem
5.0.1
to a computation in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(H)

As in Theorem
5.0.1
, let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve, let
H
be a smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N\geq 1
, and let

f:H\longrightarrow C

be a proper surjective morphism with a finite subset
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate.

Let
\Delta:=f(\Sigma)
be the set-theoretic image of
\Sigma
, that we see as a reduced divisor in
C
. We can rephrase the hypothesis as the divisor in
H
:

H_{\Delta}:=f^{\ast}\Delta

having only finitely many singularities, all of which are ordinary double points.

Let

\nu:\tilde{H}\longrightarrow H

be the blow-up of
H
at
\Sigma
, and let
E
be the exceptional divisor. Let

g:=f\circ\nu:\tilde{H}\longrightarrow C

be its composition with
f
. Over the generic point
\eta
of
C
, this morphism can be identified with the morphism
f
.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, let us define a subscheme in
\tilde{H}
by:

E_{P}:=\nu^{-1}(\{P\}),

so that the
(E_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
are the connected components of the exceptional divisor
E
. In particular, they are disjoint divisors, isomorphic to the complex projective space
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
; see for instance Proposition
5.2.1
below.

Let us define a divisor in
\tilde{H}
by:

\tilde{H}_{\Delta}:=g^{\ast}\Delta=\nu^{\ast}H_{\Delta}.

Since the divisor
H_{\Delta}
has its only singularities at points of
\Sigma
, and since all of these singularities are ordinary double points, its pullback divisor
\tilde{H}_{\Delta}
can be written as follows:

(5.1.1)

\tilde{H}_{\Delta}=2\sum_{P\in\Sigma}E_{P}+W

where
W
is the proper transform in
\tilde{H}
of the divisor
H_{\Delta}
in
H
; it is a non-singular divisor intersecting transversally the components
(E_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
of the exceptional divisor, and for every point
P
, the intersection
E_{P}\cap W
is a smooth quadric in the projective space
E_{P}
.

In particular, the divisor
\tilde{H}_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings, which allows us to define the relative logarithmic bundle
\omega^{1}_{\tilde{H}/C}
.

We are going to apply Theorem
4.2.3
to the morphism
g
and to the variations of Hodge structures of relative cohomology:

\mathbb{H}^{n}({H}_{\eta}/C_{\eta})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{H}^{n}(\widetilde{H}_{\eta}/C_{\eta}).

For this, we are going to prove the following lemma using the results of Chapter
3
.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

With the above notation, the following formula holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(H)
:

(5.1.2)

\nu_{\ast}\big((c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\widetilde{H}/C})\big)=(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}[\Sigma].

Let us prove Theorem
5.0.1
using this lemma.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the complex scheme
E_{P}
is isomorphic to the projective space
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
, hence its topological Euler characteristic is given by:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{P})=N.

Furthermore, the intersection
E_{P}\cap W
is isomorphic to a smooth quadric in
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
, hence its topological Euler characteristic is given by:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{P}\cap W)=\frac{1}{2}[(-1)^{N}+2N-1].

This follows for instance from equalities (
5.2.5
) and (
5.2.3
) in Proposition
5.2.1
below, applied to the integers
m_{P}:=2
and
r:=N-2
and the hypersurface
Q_{P}:=E_{P}\cap W
, and from equality (
5.3.1
) in Proposition
5.3.1
below, applied to the integers
n:=N
and
r:=2
and the complex number
a:=2
.

By additivity of the topological Euler characteristic, the characteristic of the complement
E_{P}-E_{P}\cap W
is given by:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{P}-E_{P}\cap W)=N-\frac{1}{2}[(-1)^{N}+2N-1]=\frac{1}{2}[1-(-1)^{N}].

Consequently, for every point
x
in
\Delta
, the expression (
4.2.2
) for the rational number
\alpha_{x}
introduced in Theorem
4.2.3
reduces to:

\displaystyle\alpha_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{N-1}{4}\sum_{P\in\Sigma_{x}}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{P}-E_{P}\cap W)+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{P\in\Sigma_{x}}\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E_{P}\cap W),

\displaystyle=\frac{N-1}{8}[1-(-1)^{N}]|\Sigma_{x}|+\frac{1}{48}[(-1)^{N}+2N-1]|\Sigma_{x}|,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{48}[6(N-1)(1-(-1)^{N})+(-1)^{N}+2N-1]|\Sigma_{x}|,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{48}[(6N-7)(1-(-1)^{N})+2N]|\Sigma_{x}|.

Hence replacing in Theorem
4.2.3
and using Lemma
5.1.1
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}g_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{Y/C})+\sum_{x\in\Delta}\alpha_{x}[x],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-\frac{1}{12}(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{48}[(6N-7)(1-(-1)^{N})+2N]\sum_{x\in\Delta}|\Sigma_{x}|[x],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])+u_{N}^{-}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

where
u_{N}^{-}
is the rational number given by:

\displaystyle u_{N}^{-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{24}(N-2)(1-(-1)^{N})+\frac{1}{48}[(6N-7)(1-(-1)^{N})+2N],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{48}[-2(N-2)(1-(-1)^{N})+(6N-7)(1-(-1)^{N})+2N],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{48}[(4N-3)(1-(-1)^{N})+2N],

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(5N-3)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
N/24&\text{if $N$ is even},\end{cases}

which shows equality (
5.0.1
).

Furthermore, for every integer
n
, equality (
2.4.2
) from Corollary
2.4.2
gives the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))+\delta^{n,N-1}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

where
\eta_{N}
is
1
if
N
is odd and
0
if
N
is even.

Taking the alternating sum over
n
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))

\displaystyle+(-1)^{N}\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])+u_{N}^{-}f_{\ast}[\Sigma]-\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])+u_{N}^{+}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

where
u_{N}^{+}
is the rational number given by:

u_{N}^{+}=u_{N}^{-}-\eta_{N}\frac{N-1}{2}=\begin{cases}-(7N-9)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
N/24&\text{if $N$ is even},\end{cases}

which shows equality (
5.0.2
) and completes the proof of Theorem
5.0.1
.

The rest of this chapter shall be devoted to proving Lemma
5.1.1
.

5.2.
Blowing up Chern classes of relative tangent classes over a curve

We shall use the following notation. Let
k
be a field,
H
a smooth
k
-scheme of pure dimension
N\geq 1
and
\Sigma
a finite subset of
H
, and let:

\nu:\tilde{H}\longrightarrow H

be the blow-up of
H
at
\Sigma
, and
E
its exceptional divisor.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, let us define a subscheme in
\tilde{H}
by:

E_{P}:=\nu^{-1}(\{P\}),

so that the
(E_{P})_{P\in\Sigma}
are the connected components of the exceptional divisor
E
. In particular, they are disjoint divisors.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, let us denote the class of the divisor
E_{P}
by:

\eta_{P}:=[E_{P}]=c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}}(E_{P}))\in{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\tilde{H}).

Observe that for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the restriction
\nu_{\mid E_{P}}:E_{P}\rightarrow H
factors through the zero-dimensional scheme
P
. Therefore, for every cycle
\alpha
in
H
of positive codimension, the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(\tilde{H})
:

(5.2.1)

\eta_{P}\cdot\nu^{\ast}\alpha=0.

Furthermore, for every pair
(P,P^{\prime})
in
\Sigma^{2}
such that
P\neq P^{\prime}
, the divisors
E_{P}
and
E_{P^{\prime}}
in
\tilde{H}
are disjoint, so we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(\tilde{H})
:

(5.2.2)

\eta_{P}\cdot\eta_{P^{\prime}}=0.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
P
be a point in
\Sigma
. The scheme
E_{P}
is isomorphic to a projective space of dimension
N-1
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(H)
:

(5.2.3)

\nu_{\ast}\eta_{P}^{N}=(-1)^{N-1}[P].

Moreover, for every non-negative integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(E_{P})
:

(5.2.4)

c_{r}(T_{E_{P}})=(-1)^{r}\binom{N}{r}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{r}.

Finally, let
m_{P}
be a positive integer, and let
Q_{P}
be a smooth hypersurface of degree
m_{P}
in
E_{P}
and let
i_{Q_{P}}:Q_{P}\rightarrow E_{P}
be the inclusion morphism.
For every non-negative integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r+1}(E_{P})
:

(5.2.5)

i_{Q_{P}\ast}c_{r}(T_{Q_{P}})=(-1)^{r+1}m_{P}\bigg[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+m_{P}y}\bigg]^{[r]}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{r+1},

where
f(y)^{[r]}
denotes the coefficient of
y^{r}
in some formal series
f(y)\in\mathbb{C}[[y]]
.

Proof.

Since
E_{P}
is a connected component of the exceptional divisor
E
, there is a canonical isomorphism of
k
-schemes:

\varphi:E_{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{k}(T_{P}H),

and a canonical isomorphism of line bundles on
E_{P}
:

\varphi^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(-1)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}}(E_{P})_{|E_{P}}.

Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(E_{P})
:

(5.2.6)

\eta_{P|E_{P}}=\varphi^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(-1)).

Now, let us compute in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

\displaystyle\eta_{P}^{N}

\displaystyle=\eta_{P}^{N-1}[E_{P}],

\displaystyle=i_{E_{P}\ast}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{N-1}\textrm{ by the projection formula,}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N-1}\,i_{E_{P}\ast}\varphi^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))^{N-1}.

Hence pushing forward, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(H)
:

\displaystyle\nu_{\ast}\eta_{P}^{N}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N-1}\,\nu_{\ast}i_{E_{P}\ast}\varphi^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))^{N-1},

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N-1}\,\Big[\int_{\mathbb{P}(T_{P}H)}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))^{N-1}\Big][P],

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N-1}\,[P],

which shows equality (
5.2.3
).

Let
r
be a non-negative integer. Since
\varphi
is an isomorphism, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(E_{P})
:

c_{r}(T_{E_{P}})=\varphi^{\ast}c_{r}(T_{\mathbb{P}(T_{P}H)}).

Using the exact sequence of vector bundles on
\mathbb{P}(T_{P}H)
from
[
Ful98
, B.5.8]
:

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}(T_{P}H)}\longrightarrow T_{P}H\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1)\longrightarrow T_{\mathbb{P}(T_{P}H)}\longrightarrow 0,

we can replace:

c_{r}(T_{E_{P}})=\varphi^{\ast}[(1+c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1)))^{N}]^{(r)}=\binom{N}{r}\varphi^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))^{r}=(-1)^{r}\binom{N}{r}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{r}\textrm{ using \eqref{eq eta_P c_1(O(1))}, }

which shows equality (
5.2.4
).

Now, let
m_{P}
be a positive integer and
Q_{P}
be an hypersurface of degree
m_{P}
in
E_{P}
.

We have the following exact sequence of vector bundles on
Q_{P}
:

0\longrightarrow T_{Q_{P}}\longrightarrow T_{E_{P}|Q_{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{Q_{P}}E_{P}\longrightarrow 0,

hence the following equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(Q_{P})
:

\displaystyle c(T_{Q_{P}})

\displaystyle=\frac{c(T_{E_{P}|Q_{P}})}{c({\mathcal{N}}_{Q_{P}}E_{P})},

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi_{|Q_{P}}^{\ast}(1+c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1)))^{N}}{1+c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E_{P}}(Q_{P})_{|Q_{P}})},

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi_{|Q_{P}}^{\ast}(1+c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1)))^{N}}{1+m_{P}\varphi_{|Q_{P}}^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))}\textrm{ because $Q_{P}$ is of degree $m_{P}$,}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{|Q_{P}}^{\ast}\bigg[\frac{(1+c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1)))^{N}}{1+m_{P}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))}\bigg].

Hence, for every integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(Q_{P})
:

(5.2.7)

c_{r}(T_{Q_{P}})=\bigg[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+m_{P}y}\bigg]^{[r]}\varphi_{|Q_{P}}^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(1))^{r}=(-1)^{r}\bigg[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+m_{P}y}\bigg]^{[r]}\eta_{P|Q_{P}}^{r}\textrm{ using \eqref{eq eta_P c_1(O(1))}.}

Pushing forward (
5.2.7
) by the inclusion map of
Q_{P}
in
E_{P}
, and using the projection formula and the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(E_{P})
:

[Q_{P}]=\varphi^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{P}H}(m_{P}))=-m_{P}\eta_{P|E_{P}},

yields equality (
5.2.5
).
∎

Recall the following consequence of the “Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch without denominators” formula:

Proposition 5.2.2

(
[
Ful98
, Example 15.4.2, (c)]
)
.

We have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(\tilde{H})
:

(5.2.8)

c(T_{\tilde{H}})=\nu^{\ast}c(T_{H})+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big].

Corollary 5.2.3
.

With the above notation, let
C
be a smooth projective
k
-curve, let

f:H\longrightarrow C

be a flat morphism, and let

g:=f\circ\nu:\tilde{H}\longrightarrow C

be its composition with the blow-down morphism
\nu
. The following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(\tilde{H})
:

c([T_{g}])=\nu^{\ast}c([T_{f}])+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big].

Proof.

By definition of the relative tangent class
[T_{g}]
in
K^{0}(\tilde{H})
, the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(\tilde{H})
:

\displaystyle c([T_{g}])

\displaystyle=c(T_{\tilde{H}}).g^{*}c(T_{C})^{-1}

(5.2.9)

\displaystyle=\big(\nu^{*}c(T_{H})+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big]\big).g^{*}c(T_{C})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\nu^{*}(c(T_{H}).f^{*}c(T_{C})^{-1})+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big].(1-g^{*}c_{1}(T_{C}))

(5.2.10)

\displaystyle=\nu^{*}(c(T_{H}).f^{*}c(T_{C})^{-1})+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big]

(5.2.11)

\displaystyle=\nu^{*}c([T_{f}])+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\big[(1+\eta_{P})(1-\eta_{P})^{N}-1\big],

where in (
5.2.9
) we used Proposition
5.2.2
, in (
5.2.10
) we applied equality (
5.2.1
) to the cycle of codimension one
\alpha:=f^{*}c_{1}(T_{C})
and used that the polynomial
(1+x)(1-x)^{N}-1
is a multiple of
x,
and in (
5.2.11
) we used the definition of the relative tangent class
[T_{f}]
in
K^{0}(H).

∎

The following corollary is a reformulation of Corollary
5.2.3
, and we leave its derivation from Corollary
5.2.3
to the reader.

Corollary 5.2.4
.

With the same notation as in Corollary
5.2.3
, for every positive integer
r,
the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

c_{r}([T_{g}])=\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])+(-1)^{r}\Bigg[\binom{N}{r}-\binom{N}{r-1}\Bigg]\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{r}.

5.3.
The Chern classes
c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\widetilde{H}/C})
in terms of
\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])

In order to prove Lemma
5.1.1
, we want to compare the cycle classes
\nu_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})
and
(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])
on
H
.

Using Corollary
5.2.3
,
we can compare the cycle classes
c_{r}([T_{g}])
and
\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])
on
\tilde{H}
, where
r
is any positive integer.
In this Section, we shall apply equality (
3.1.12
) from Proposition
3.1.7
to compare the cycle classes
c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})
and
c_{r}([T_{g}])
on
\tilde{H}
(this will be the object of Proposition
5.3.2
), then we shall combine this with Corollary
5.2.3
to compare the cycle classes
c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})
and
\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])
(this will be the object of Corollary
5.3.3
). Next, we shall apply these expressions with the integers
r:=1
and
r:=N-1
to compare the cycle classes
(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})
and
\nu^{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])
(this will be the object of Corollary
5.3.4
). After pushing forward by
\nu
, this will establish Lemma
5.1.1
and complete the proof of Theorem
5.0.1
.

If
y
denotes an indeterminate and
f(y)
a formal series in
\mathbb{C}[[y]]
, for every integer
p
, we shall denote by
f(y)^{[p]}
its coefficient of degree
p
. It vanishes if
p
is negative.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

For every
a
in
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
, and every
n
and
r
in
\mathbb{N}
, we have the formulas:

(5.3.1)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n}}{1+ay}\right]^{[n-r]}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}\sum_{r\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{k}(-1)^{k}a^{k-r}=\frac{(-1)^{n+r}}{a^{r}}\big[(a-1)^{n}-\sum_{0\leq k\leq r-1}\binom{n}{k}(-1)^{n-k}a^{k}\big],

and:

(5.3.2)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{(1+ay)^{2}}\right]^{[n-r]}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r+1}\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n+1}(k-r)\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{k}a^{k-r-1}

(5.3.3)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n+r}}{a^{r+1}}\Big[\big(r+(n+1-r)a\big)(a-1)^{n}-\sum_{0\leq k\leq r}(k-r)\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{n+1-k}a^{k}\Big].

Proof.

The equality between the left-hand side and the middle side in (
5.3.1
) follows from the computation:

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n}}{1+ay}\right]^{[n-r]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq i\leq n-r}[(1+y)^{n}]^{[i]}\Big[\frac{1}{1+ay}\Big]^{[n-r-i]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq i\leq n-r}\binom{n}{i}(-a)^{n-r-i}

(5.3.4)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{n-k}(-a)^{k-r}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r}\sum_{r\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{k}(-1)^{k}a^{k-r},

where in (
5.3.4
) we have introduced
k:=n-i
.

The equality between the middle side and the right-hand side in (
5.3.1
) follows from the binomial formula applied to
(a-1)^{n}
.

Equality (
5.3.2
) follows from the equality of formal series in
\mathbb{C}[[a,y]]
:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}a}\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{1+ay}\right]=-y\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{(1+ay)^{2}},

which implies the equality of complex numbers:

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{(1+ay)^{2}}\right]^{[n-r]}

\displaystyle=-\bigg[y^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}a}\left(\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{1+ay}\right)\bigg]^{[n-r]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}a}\left[\frac{(1+y)^{n+1}}{1+ay}\right]^{[n+1-r]}

(5.3.5)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}a}\big[(-1)^{r}\sum_{r\leq k\leq n+1}\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{k}a^{k-r}\big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r+1}\sum_{r+1\leq k\leq n+1}(k-r)\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{k}a^{k-r-1},

where in (
5.3.5
), we have used (
5.3.1
) applied to
n^{\prime}:=n+1
.

Equality (
5.3.3
) follows from the expansion:

\displaystyle\big(r+(n+1-r)a\big)(a-1)^{n}

\displaystyle=(n+1)a(a-1)^{n}-r(a-1)^{n+1}

\displaystyle=(n+1)\sum_{1\leq k\leq n+1}\binom{n}{k-1}(-1)^{n-k+1}a^{k}

\displaystyle-r\sum_{0\leq k\leq n+1}\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{n+1-k}a^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n+1}\bigg((n+1)\binom{n}{k-1}-r\binom{n+1}{k}\bigg)(-1)^{n+1-k}a^{k}

(5.3.6)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n+1}(k-r)\binom{n+1}{k}(-1)^{n+1-k}a^{k},

where in (
5.3.6
), we have used the classical identity, for every integer
k
:

(n+1)\binom{n}{k-1}=k\binom{n+1}{k}.\qed

Let us adopt the notation of Section
5.1
.

For every point
P
in
\Sigma
, let us denote the class of the divisor
E_{P}
by:

\eta_{P}:=[E_{P}]=c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{H}}(E_{P}))\in{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\tilde{H}).

Observe that equalities (
5.2.1
) and (
5.2.2
) still hold in this setting.

Proposition 5.3.2
.

With the above notation, for every non-negative integer
r
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\alpha(N,r)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{r},

where
\alpha(N,r)
is the integer given by:

\alpha(N,r):=(-1)^{r-1}\Bigg(\binom{N}{r-1}-4\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[r-2]}\Bigg).

Observe that the integer
\alpha(N,1)
is simply 1.

Proof.

Let us apply equality (
3.1.12
) from Proposition
3.1.7
to the scheme

Y:=\tilde{H},

and to the morphism
g
, whose divisor of singular fibers
\tilde{H}_{\Delta}
is of the form given by (
5.1.1
). Observe that with the notation of that proposition, the subscheme
D^{3}
is empty.

We obtain, for every integer
r
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

(5.3.7)

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=c_{r}([T_{g}])+\sum_{P\in\Sigma}i_{E_{P}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{E_{P}})-2\sum_{P\in\Sigma}i_{E_{P}\cap W\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{E_{P}\cap W}).

Using (
5.2.4
) from Proposition
5.2.1
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r-1}(E_{P})
:

c_{r-1}(T_{E_{P}})=(-1)^{r-1}\binom{N}{r-1}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{r-1},

hence pushing forward by the inclusion in
\tilde{H}
and using the projection formula, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

i_{E_{P}\ast}c_{r-1}(T_{E_{P}})=(-1)^{r-1}\binom{N}{r-1}\eta_{P}^{r}.

On the other hand, the intersection
Q_{P}:=E_{P}\cap W
is a smooth hypersurface of degree
m_{P}:=2
in the projective space
E_{P}
, hence using equality (
5.2.5
) from Proposition
5.2.1
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r-1}(E_{P})
:

i_{E_{P}\cap W\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{E_{P}\cap W})=2(-1)^{r-1}\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[r-2]}\eta_{P|E_{P}}^{r-1},

hence pushing forward by the inclusion in
\tilde{H}
and using the projection formula, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

i_{E_{P}\cap W\ast}c_{r-2}(T_{E_{P}\cap W})=2(-1)^{r-1}\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[r-2]}\eta_{P}^{r}.

Replacing in (
5.3.7
) yields the result.
∎

Corollary 5.3.3
.

With the above notation, for every positive integer
r
, the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])+\beta(N,r)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{r},

where
\beta(N,r)
is the integer given by:

\beta(N,r):=(-1)^{r}\bigg[\binom{N}{r}-2\binom{N}{r-1}+4\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[r-2]}\bigg].

Observe that the integer
\beta(N,1)
is equal to
-N+2.

Proof.

For every positive integer
r
, combining Corollary
5.2.4
and Proposition
5.3.2
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{r}(\tilde{H})
:

c_{r}(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=\nu^{\ast}c_{r}([T_{f}])+\gamma(N,r)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{r},

where
\gamma(N,r)
is the integer given by:

\displaystyle\gamma(N,r)

\displaystyle=\alpha(N,r)+(-1)^{r}\Bigg[\binom{N}{r}-\binom{N}{r-1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r-1}\Bigg[\binom{N}{r-1}-4\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[r-2]}\Bigg]+(-1)^{r}\Bigg[\binom{N}{r}-\binom{N}{r-1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\beta(N,r).\qed

Corollary 5.3.4
.

With the above notation, the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H)}
:

(5.3.8)
