(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=\nu^{*}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N}.

Proof.

If the integer
N
is 1, this follows directly from Corollary
5.3.3
applied to the integer
r:=1
(observe that the second term in the right-hand side of (
5.3.8
) is
\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}
).

Let us assume that the integer
N
is at least 2.
Applying Corollary
5.3.3
to the positive integers
r=1
and
r=N-1,
then multiplying, yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

\displaystyle(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})

\displaystyle=(\nu^{*}c_{1}([T_{f}])+\beta(N,1)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}).(\nu^{*}c_{N-1}([T_{f}])+\beta(N,N-1)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N-1})

\displaystyle=\nu^{*}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])+\beta(N,N-1)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N-1}\nu^{*}c_{1}([T_{f}])

(5.3.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta(N,1)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}\nu^{*}c_{N-1}([T_{f}])+\beta(N,1)\beta(N,N-1)\sum_{(P,P^{\prime})\in\Sigma^{2}}\eta_{P}\eta_{P^{\prime}}^{N-1}.

Since we have assumed that
N
was at least
2
, we can apply equality (
5.2.1
) to the cycle
\alpha:=c_{N-1}([T_{f}])
of positive codimension to obtain, for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

\eta_{P}\,\nu^{\ast}c_{N-1}([T_{f}])=0.

Furthermore, we can apply this same equality to the cycle
\alpha:=c_{1}([T_{f}])
to obtain, for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

\eta_{P}^{N-1}\,\nu^{\ast}c_{1}([T_{f}])=\eta_{P}^{N-2}\big(\eta_{P}\,\nu^{\ast}c_{1}([T_{f}])\big)=0.

Finally, applying equality (
5.2.2
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}\,\eta_{P^{\prime}}^{N-1}=\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N}.

Hence replacing in (
5.3.9
):

(5.3.10)

(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=\nu^{*}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])+\beta(N,1)\beta(N,N-1)\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N}.

Now let us compute the combinatorial coefficient
\beta(N,1)\beta(N,N-1).
Applying equality (
5.3.1
) from Proposition
5.3.1
to the integers
r=3
and
n=N
and to the complex number
a=2
yields the equality:

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\right]^{[N-3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N+3}}{2^{3}}\Big[(2-1)^{N}-\sum_{0\leq k\leq 2}\binom{N}{k}(-1)^{N-k}2^{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{8}\big[1+(-1)^{N+1}+2N(-1)^{N}+4N(N-1)/2(-1)^{N+1}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{8}\big[1+(-1)^{N+1}(1-2N+2N(N-1))\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\big[(-1)^{N+1}+2N^{2}-4N+1\big].

Consequently, the combinatorial coefficient
\beta(N,1)\beta(N,N-1)
is given by:

\displaystyle\beta(N,1)\beta(N,N-1)

\displaystyle=(-N+2)(-1)^{N-1}\bigg[\binom{N}{N-1}-2\binom{N}{N-2}+4\Big[\frac{(1+y)^{N}}{1+2y}\Big]^{[N-3]}\bigg]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}(N-2)\big[N-2N(N-1)/2+4/8[(-1)^{N+1}+2N^{2}-4N+1]\big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}(N-2)\big[N-N(N-1)+(1+(-1)^{N+1})/2+N^{2}-2N\big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}(N-2)(1+(-1)^{N+1})/2

\displaystyle=-(N-2)(1-(-1)^{N})/2.

Replacing this expression in (
5.3.10
) yields the result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1.1
.

By Corollary
5.3.4
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(\tilde{H})
:

(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})=\nu^{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}\sum_{P\in\Sigma}\eta_{P}^{N},

hence pushing forward by the morphism
\nu
, whose generic degree is one, and using equality (
5.2.3
) from Proposition
5.2.1
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(H)
:

\displaystyle\nu_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})(\omega^{1\vee}_{\tilde{H}/C})

\displaystyle=(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}(-1)^{N-1}[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])-(N-2)\frac{1-(-1)^{N}}{2}[\Sigma],

as wanted.
∎

Chapter 6
The Griffiths height of the middle-dimensional cohomology of a pencil of hypersurfaces

6.1.
Families of ample divisors in a smooth pencil

The objective of this section is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
,
X
be a smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N+1
, and let

\pi:X\longrightarrow C

be a smooth surjective morphism of complex schemes. Let
H
be a non-singular hypersurface in
X
such that the morphism

\pi_{|H}:H\longrightarrow C

is flat
1
1

1

or equivalently, is non-constant on every connected component of
H
, or has fibers of pure dimension
N-1
.
and has a finite set
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate.

If we denote by
L
the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(H)
on
X
, then the following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

(6.1.1)

[\Sigma]=\big((1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big)^{(N+1)},

If moreover the line bundle
L
is ample relatively to
\pi
,
then the following equalities hold in the Chow group with rational coefficients
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.1.2)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))\\
=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(X/C)))+c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N+1}(X/C)))-c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N}(X/C)))\\
+\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\Big[\big((1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big)^{(N+1)}\Big]-\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\big(c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big)+v^{+}_{N}\,\pi_{\ast}[\Sigma],

and:

(6.1.3)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))\\
=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(X/C)))+c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N+1}(X/C)))-c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N}(X/C)))\\
+\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\Big[\big((1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big)^{(N+1)}\Big]-\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\big(c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big)+v^{-}_{N}\,\pi_{\ast}[\Sigma],

where:

v^{+}_{N}:=\begin{cases}7(N-1)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
(N+2)/24&\text{if $N$ is even}.\end{cases}

and:

v^{-}_{N}:=\begin{cases}-5(N-1)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
(N+2)/24&\text{if $N$ is even}.\end{cases}

6.1.1.
Computation of
i_{\ast}[\Sigma]
and of
i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
, let
X
be a smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N+1
, and let

\pi:X\longrightarrow C

be a smooth surjective morphism of complex schemes. Let
H
be a non-singular hypersurface in
X
such that the morphism

f:=\pi_{|H}:H\longrightarrow C

is flat and has a finite set
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate.

Let

i:H\longrightarrow X

be the inclusion map. Let us also denote by
L
the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(H)
on
X
.

Observe that the line bundle
L^{\vee}_{|H}
on
H
can be identified with the conormal line bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{H}X^{\vee}
of the hypersurface
H
in
X
.

Let us define a map
s
of vector bundles on
H
by the composition:

s:L^{\vee}_{|H}\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{H}X^{\vee}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X|H}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}.

We will also see
s
as a section of the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\otimes L_{|H}
.

Some of the content of the upcoming results, Lemmas
6.1.2
and
6.1.3
, can be summarized in the commutative diagram below, where all the horizontal and vertical sequences are exact sequences of coherent sheaves on
H
:

(6.1.4)

\textstyle{f^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{t}(D\pi)}
\textstyle{f^{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{t}(Df)}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}}_{H}X^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X\mid H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{t}(Di)}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}}_{H}X^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X/C\mid H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{t}(Di)}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{H/C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

In (
6.1.4
),
{}^{t}(Df)
(resp.
{}^{t}(D\pi)
,
{}^{t}(Di)
) denotes the morphism between coherent sheaves of Kähler differentials induced by the morphism of schemes
f
(resp.
\pi
,
i
).

Lemma 6.1.2
.

The section
s
does not vanish on the open dense subset
H-\Sigma
in
H
, and for every point
P
in
\Sigma
, there exists a system
(z_{1},...,z_{N})
of local analytic coordinates on
H
near
P
such that, in some local frame of the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\otimes L_{|H}
, the section
s
is given by:

s=(2z_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}.

Proof.

By definition of the subset
\Sigma
, the morphism of non-singular complex schemes
f
is smooth on the open dense subset
H-\Sigma
, so that by elementary properties of the sheaf of Kähler differentials, the section
s
does not vanish on
H-\Sigma
.

Now, let
P
be a point in
\Sigma
, and let
y
in
C
be its image by
f
. The hypersurfaces
H
and
X_{y}
in
X
are both non-singular and meet in
P
. By definition of
\Sigma
, the morphism
f
has a critical point in
P
, so we have the equality of hyperplanes in
T_{X,P}
:

(6.1.5)

T_{H,P}=T_{X_{y},P}

Let
t
be a local coordinate of
C
in some analytic neighborhood of
y
. Since the morphism
\pi
is smooth, we can extend the function
\pi^{\ast}t
into a local coordinate system
(\pi^{\ast}t,z_{1},...,z_{N})
in some analytic neighborhood
V
of
P
.

Using (
6.1.5
) and the implicit function theorem, after possibly shrinking
V
, there exists a diffeomorphism:

\varphi:X_{y}\cap V\longrightarrow H\cap V

which sends
P
to
P
and whose differential at
P
is the identity map

{\mathrm{Id}}:T_{X_{y},P}\longrightarrow T_{H,P}.

In other terms, there exists an analytic neighborhood
U
of
0
in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, and a function:

u:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{C},

vanishing in the origin, and whose derivative vanishes in the origin, and such that
H\cap V
is defined by the equation:

(6.1.6)

\pi^{\ast}t=u(z_{1},...,z_{N}).

The morphism
\varphi
and the function
u
are related by the equality of morphisms from
X_{y}
to
\mathbb{C}^{N+1}
:

(6.1.7)

(\pi^{\ast}t,z_{1},...,z_{N})\circ\varphi=(u(z_{1},...,z_{N}),z_{1},...,z_{N}).

Observe that
H
admits a local coordinate system given by
(z_{1|H},...,z_{N|H})
.

By hypothesis,
y
is a non-degenerate critical point of the morphism
f=\pi_{|H}
on
H
, hence it is also a non-degenerate critical point of the morphism:

\pi_{|H\cap V}\circ\varphi:X_{y}\cap V\longrightarrow C.

Using (
6.1.7
), this morphism is given in local coordinates by:

(\pi^{\ast}t)_{|H\cap V}\circ\varphi=u(z_{1},...,z_{N}),

so we obtain that the Hessian at
0
of the function
u
is non-degenerate. Consequently, after possibly shrinking the neighborhood
V
and changing the coordinates
z_{1},...,z_{N}
, we can assume that the function
u
is given by:

(6.1.8)

u(z_{1},...,z_{N})=z_{1}^{2}+...+z_{N}^{2}.

The vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X}
admits a local frame given by
(\pi^{\ast}\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}z_{1},...,\mathrm{d}z_{N})
. Using that the hypersurface
H
is defined by the equation (
6.1.6
), the conormal line bundle
L_{|H}^{\vee}\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{H}X^{\vee}
, as embedded in the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X|H}
, is generated by the non-vanishing section:

s_{0}:=\frac{\partial u}{\partial z_{1}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}+...+\frac{\partial u}{\partial z_{N}}\mathrm{d}z_{N}-\pi^{\ast}\mathrm{d}t=2z_{1|H}\mathrm{d}z_{1}+...+2z_{N|H}\mathrm{d}z_{N}-\pi^{\ast}\mathrm{d}t\textrm{ using \eqref{u non-degenerate}}.

The vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X/C}
admits a local frame given by
([\mathrm{d}z_{1}],...,[\mathrm{d}z_{N}])
. In this frame, the image by the projection

\Omega^{1}_{X|H}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}

of the section
s_{0}
is given by:

2z_{1|H}[\mathrm{d}z_{1}]+...+2z_{N|H}[\mathrm{d}z_{N}].

This image is precisely the section
s
, which proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.1.3
.

We have an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
H
:

(6.1.9)

0\longrightarrow L^{\vee}_{|H}\overset{s}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\overset{{}^{t}(Di)}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{H/C}\longrightarrow 0,

where
{}^{t}(Di)
is the map between sheaves of Kähler differentials associated with the morphism of
C
-schemes
i
.

Furthermore, the subscheme of
H
defined by the vanishing of
s
is precisely the reduced subscheme defined by the finite subset
\Sigma
.

Proof.

For the exactness of (
6.1.9
), everything but the injectivity of the morphism
s
is a standard property of sheaves of relative Kähler differentials. By Lemma
6.1.2
, the morphism
s
does not vanish on the open dense subset
H-\Sigma
in
H
. Since both coherent sheaves
L^{\vee}_{|H}
and
\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}
are locally free on
H
, the morphism of coherent sheaves
s
is therefore injective, which completes the proof of the exactness of (
6.1.9
).

Furthermore, by Lemma
6.1.2
, the morphism
s
vanishes with order
1
in every point of
\Sigma
. Consequently, the subscheme defined by the vanishing of the morphism
s
is precisely the reduced subscheme defined by
\Sigma
, as wanted.
∎

Proposition 6.1.4
.

We have the following equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

(6.1.10)

i_{\ast}[\Sigma]=[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)},

and:

(6.1.11)

i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])=(c_{1}c_{N})([T_{\pi}])\\
+(-1)^{N}\big([(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)}+i_{\ast}[\Sigma]-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big).

Proof.

Using Lemma
6.1.3
, the subscheme defined by the vanishing of the section
s
of the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\otimes L_{|H}
on
H
is exactly the reduced subscheme defined by
\Sigma
, in particular, it is of dimension
0
, hence of codimension
N
in
H
. The rank of the vector bundle
\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\otimes L_{|H}
on
H
is exactly
N
, so that
s
is a regular section in the sense of
[
Ful98
, B.3.4]
.

Consequently, using
[
Ful98
, Example 3.2.16, (ii)]
, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(H)
:

\displaystyle[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H}\otimes L_{|H}),

\displaystyle=i^{\ast}c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}\otimes L),

\displaystyle=i^{\ast}\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

where the last expression follows from the formula for the top Chern class of the tensor product of a vector bundle by a line bundle (
[
Ful98
, Remark 3.2.3, (b)]
).

Pushing forward by the inclusion
i
and applying the projection formula, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle i_{\ast}[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=[H]\cdot\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

\displaystyle=c_{1}(L)\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

(6.1.12)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N+1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq N+1}c_{1}(L)^{N+1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\textrm{ because the vector bundle $\Omega^{1}_{X/C}$ is of rank $N$,}

\displaystyle=[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)},

which shows equality (
6.1.10
).

Now let us show equality (
6.1.11
). Using exact sequence (
6.1.9
) and the multiplicativity of total Chern classes, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{\ast}(H)
:

c([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])=c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C|H})c(L^{\vee}_{|H})^{-1}=i^{\ast}[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})].

Taking the terms of codimension
1
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(H)
:

(6.1.13)

c_{1}([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])=i^{\ast}[c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})+c_{1}(L)],

and taking the terms of codimension
N-1
, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N-1}(H)
:

(6.1.14)

c_{N-1}([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])=i^{\ast}\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}).

Multiplying (
6.1.13
) and (
6.1.14
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N}(H)\simeq{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(H)
:

\displaystyle(c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])

\displaystyle=i^{\ast}\Big[\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})+\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-1-k}(c_{1}c_{k})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\Big].

Pushing forward by
i
and applying the projection formula, we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])

\displaystyle=c_{1}(L).\Big[\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})+\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-1-k}(c_{1}c_{k})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\Big],

(6.1.15)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N+1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})+\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}(c_{1}c_{k})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}).

The first sum in (
6.1.15
) is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N+1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N+1-k}c_{k}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

\displaystyle=i_{\ast}[\Sigma]-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\textrm{ using equality \eqref{isig}
}.

Similarly, the second sum in (
6.1.15
) is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq k\leq N-1}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}(c_{1}c_{k})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq N}c_{1}(L)^{N-k}(c_{1}c_{k})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})-(c_{1}c_{N})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}),

\displaystyle=[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)}-(c_{1}c_{N})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}).

Hence replacing in (
6.1.15
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}])=i_{\ast}[\Sigma]-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})+[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)}-(c_{1}c_{N})(\Omega^{1}_{X/C}).

Since the morphism of non-singular complex schemes
f
is smooth on an open dense subset of
H
, similarly to Subsection
3.1.4
, we have the following equality in
K^{0}(H)\simeq K_{0}(H)
:

[T_{f}]=[\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]^{\vee},

where
.^{\vee}
denotes the duality involution on
K^{0}(H)
.

Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(X)
:

\displaystyle i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([T_{f}])

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N}i_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]),

\displaystyle=(c_{1}c_{N})([T_{\pi}])

\displaystyle+(-1)^{N}\big([(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)}+i_{\ast}[\Sigma]-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big),

which shows equality (
6.1.11
).
∎

6.1.2.
An application of Lefschetz’s weak theorem

We can also prove the following consequence of Lefschetz’s weak theorem and of Poincaré duality.

Proposition 6.1.5
.

If the line bundle
L
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
, then for every integer
n
such that
n<N-1
, the local monodromy of
\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
is unipotent and we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)
:

(6.1.16)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C))),

and for every integer
n
such that
n>N-1
, the local monodromy of
\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
is unipotent and we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.1.17)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n+2}(X/C))).

A minor variant of the proof below would actually show that the local monodromy of
\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
is trivial when
n\neq N-1
.

Proof.

Let us assume that the line bundle
L
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
. Let
\Delta
be the set-theoretic image:

\Delta:=f(\Sigma),

and let:

{\mathaccent 23{C}}:=C-\Delta

be its complement in
C
.

Let
n
be an integer such that
n<N-1
.

Since the line bundle
L={\mathcal{O}}_{X}(H)
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
, using Lefschetz’s weak theorem (see for instance
[
Voi03
, Theorem 1.29]
), we obtain that the pullback morphism of variations of Hodge structures on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
:

\mathbb{H}^{n}(i^{\ast}):\mathbb{H}^{n}(X-X_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{n}(H-H_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})

is an isomorphism.

Since
X
is smooth over
C
, the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(X-X_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
on
{\mathaccent 23{C}}
can be extended into the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C)
on
C
, hence its local monodromy at every point of
\Delta
is trivial.

Consequently, the local monodromy of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(H-H_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
at every point of
\Delta
is trivial, in particular unipotent, and the isomorphism of VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(i^{\ast})
extends into an isomorphism on the (upper or lower) Deligne extension.

Consequently, it induces an isomorphism of line bundles on
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(i^{\ast})):\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C))\longrightarrow\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})).

Taking Chern classes, we obtain immediately equality (
6.1.16
).

Now, let
n
be an integer such that
n>N-1
. Applying Proposition
2.5.2
to the blow-up
Y
of
H
at every point of
\Sigma
, satisfying that the divisor
Y_{\Delta}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings, and to the integer

n^{\prime}:=2(N-1)-n<N-1,

and using that the local monodromy at every point of
\Delta
of the VHS

\mathbb{H}^{n^{\prime}}(Y-Y_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
is unipotent (which we showed above), we obtain that the local monodromy at every point of
\Delta
of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y-Y_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}})
, hence of the VHS
\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
, is unipotent and that the line bundle on
C
:

\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(Y-Y_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}}))\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(Y-Y_{\Delta}/{\mathaccent 23{C}}))^{\vee}\\
\simeq\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\otimes\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))

is of
2
-torsion. Consequently, we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
,

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))).

Applying equality (
6.1.16
) to the integer
n^{\prime}:=2(N-1)-n<N-1
, we obtain:

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2(N-1)-n}(X/C))).

Applying Proposition
2.5.2
to
X
, with the set of critical values being empty, we obtain:

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{2N-(2(N-1)-n)}(X/C)))=c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n+2}(X/C))),

which shows equality (
6.1.17
).
∎

Now we can conclude the proof of Proposition
6.1.1
. Let us assume that the line bundle
L
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
.

Applying equality (
5.0.1
) from Theorem
5.0.1
to the
C
-scheme
H
, then applying equality (
6.1.11
) from Proposition
6.1.4
, we have the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}f_{\ast}\big((c_{1}c_{N-1})([\Omega^{1}_{H/C}]^{\vee})\big)+u_{N}^{-}f_{\ast}[\Sigma],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N})([T_{\pi}])+(u_{N}^{-}+\frac{(-1)^{N}}{12})f_{\ast}[\Sigma]

(6.1.18)

\displaystyle+\frac{(-1)^{N}}{12}\pi_{\ast}\big([(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})]^{(N+1)}-c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{X/C})\big),

where
u_{N}^{-}
is the rational number defined by:

u_{N}^{-}:=\begin{cases}(5N-3)/24&\text{if $N$ is odd}\\
N/24&\text{if $N$ is even}.\end{cases}

Applying Theorem
5.0.1
to the smooth
C
-scheme
X
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.1.19)

\sum_{n=0}^{2N}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C)))=\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}(c_{1}c_{N})([T_{\pi}]).

Finally, it follows from Proposition
6.1.5
that we have the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.1.20)

\sum_{n=0}^{2(N-1)}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))\big)-\sum_{n=0}^{2N}(-1)^{n-1}c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C)))\\
=(-1)^{N}[c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))-c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(X/C)))\\
+c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N}(X/C)))-c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{N+1}(X/C)))].

Equality (
6.1.3
) follows by combining equalities (
6.1.18
), (
6.1.19
) and (
6.1.20
). Equality (
6.1.2
) is proved in the same way, using equality (
5.0.2
) from Theorem
5.0.1
instead of equality (
5.0.1
).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
6.1.1
.

6.2.
Pencils of hypersurfaces in the projective space

In this section, we shall use Proposition
6.1.1
to compute the Griffiths height of the middle -dimensional cohomology of pencils of projective hypersurfaces. Before stating our results, we introduce some notation and recall some basic facts concerning projective bundles over a projective curve, their Chow groups, and their hypersurfaces.

6.2.1.
Preliminary: projective bundles over a curve and horizontal hypersurfaces

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
, let
E
be a vector bundle of rank
N+1\geq 1
over
C
, and let:

\pi:\mathbb{P}(E):={\rm Proj\,}S^{\bullet}E^{\vee}\longrightarrow C,

be the associated projective bundle over
C
.

We denote by
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)
the tautological quotient line bundle over
\mathbb{P}(E)
; it is the dual of the tautological subbundle of rank
1
of
\pi^{\ast}E
.

We shall use the fact (see for instance
[
Ful98
, B.5.8]
) that the relative tangent bundle

T_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}:=T_{\pi}

fits into a short exact sequence:

(6.2.1)

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}(E)}\longrightarrow\pi^{\ast}E\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)\longrightarrow T_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
H
be an hypersurface, namely an effective Cartier divisor, in
\mathbb{P}(E)
. It is a projective complex scheme of dimension
N
. Its generic fiber
H_{\eta}
is an hypersurface in the projective space
\mathbb{P}(E)_{\eta}
of dimension
N
over
\mathbb{C}(C)
. We shall denote its degree by
d
.

We shall also denote by:

i:H\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(E)

the inclusion morphism of
H
in the projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(E)
, and by:

f:=\pi\circ i:H\longrightarrow C

its projection to the curve
C
.

As is well-known, the hypersurface
H_{\eta}
in the projective space
\mathbb{P}(E)_{\eta}
may be defined, as a subscheme, by the vanishing of an homogeneous polynomial of degree
d
— namely by a non-zero element of
S^{d}E^{\vee}_{\eta}
— unique up to the multiplication by an element of
\mathbb{C}(C)^{\ast}.

This description of
H_{\eta}
extends to a description of the “relative hypersurface”
H
over
C
as follows.

Since
\mathbb{P}(E)
is a regular scheme, its Picard group is isomorphic to its Chow group of codimension one
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}(E))
. According to the structure of the Chow groups of a projective bundle (see
[
Ful98
, Th. 3.3 (b)]
, with
k=\dim(\mathbb{P}(E))-1
), there is an isomorphism:

\mathbb{Z}\oplus{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}(E)),\quad(\delta,\alpha)\longmapsto\delta c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1))+\pi^{\ast}\alpha.

Consequently the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}(E)}(H)
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(\delta)\otimes\pi^{\ast}M
for some integer
\delta
and some line bundle
M
over
C
. By considering the restriction of this isomorphism to
\mathbb{P}(E)_{\eta}
, we see that
\delta
coincides with the generic degree
d
of
H
. Moreover the line bundle
M
is unique up to isomorphism.

We will denote by:

(6.2.2)

\sigma:{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}(E)}(H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(d)\otimes\pi^{\ast}M

the isomorphism obtained by this construction. This isomorphism may be seen as a morphism of line bundles over
\mathbb{P}(E)
:

(6.2.3)

\sigma:\pi^{\ast}M^{\vee}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(d),

the vanishing of which defines
H
scheme-theoretically. In turn, the data of the morphism (
6.2.3
) is equivalent to the data of a morphism of sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
-modules:

(6.2.4)

\tau:M^{\vee}\longrightarrow\pi_{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(d)\simeq S^{d}E^{\vee},

which is clearly injective.

For every point
x
of
C
, the image
\tau_{x}(y)
of any non-zero element
y
of
M^{\vee}_{x}
is an element of
S^{d}E^{\vee}_{x}
, which is a (scheme-theoretic) equation for the fiber
H_{x}
of
H
in the projective space
\mathbb{P}(E)_{x}
over
\mathbb{C}
.
This shows that the hypersurface
H
is horizontal — namely, that no fiber of
\pi
is a component of
H
, or equivalently that the morphism
f:H\rightarrow C
is flat — if and only if
\tau_{x}
is non-zero for every point
x
of
C
, or equivalently if and only if
\tau
is an isomorphism onto a locally direct summand of
S^{d}E^{\vee}
.

In the sequel, we shall use the following notation for the first Chern classes of
E
and
M
, and of
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)
, in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)
and
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}(E))
respectively:

e:=c_{1}(E),\quad m:=c_{1}(M),\quad\mbox{and}\quad h:=c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)).

Using the isomorphism (
6.2.2
), we obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}(E))
:

(6.2.5)

[H]=d\,h+\pi^{\ast}m.

Using the definition of Segre classes and their relation to Chern classes (
[
Ful98
, 3.1, 3.2]
), and the fact that
C
is one-dimensional, we obtain the following equality, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
:

(6.2.6)

\pi_{\ast}h^{i}=:s_{i-N}(E)=\begin{cases}0&\quad\mbox{if }i<N,\\
[C]&\quad\mbox{if }i=N,\\
-e&\quad\mbox{if }i=N+1,\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }i>N+1.\end{cases}

From the relations above and the projection formula, we deduce:

(6.2.7)

\pi_{\ast}(h^{N-1}\cap[H])=d[C],

and

(6.2.8)

\pi_{\ast}(h^{N}\cap[H])=-de+m.

In computations, we will use that
{\mathrm{CH}}^{i}(C)
vanishes for
i>1
; in particular, we have:

(6.2.9)

m\cdot m=e\cdot e=m\cdot e=0.

Remark 6.2.1
.

The classes
m
and
e
depend on the choice of the bundle
E
, and not only on the
C
-scheme
\mathbb{P}{}(E)
.

More specifically, if
L
is a line bundle on
C
, and
E
is replaced by
E^{\prime}:=E\otimes L
, then we may identify the
C
-schemes
\mathbb{P}(E^{\prime})
and
\mathbb{P}(E)
, but
e
is replaced by:

e^{\prime}:=c_{1}(E^{\prime})=c_{1}(E\otimes L)=e+(N+1)\textrm{ }c_{1}(L).

The line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)
is replaced by:

{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}(1)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)\otimes\pi^{\ast}L^{\vee},

and the class
h
is replaced by:

h^{\prime}:=c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}(1))=h-\pi^{\ast}c_{1}(L).

Moreover, the bundle
S^{d}E^{\prime\vee}
may be identified with
(S^{d}E^{\vee})\otimes L^{-d}
. The morphism of vector bundles:

\tau^{\prime}:=\tau\otimes{\mathrm{Id}}_{L^{-d}}:M^{\vee}\otimes L^{-d}\hookrightarrow S^{d}E^{\prime\vee}

clearly represents the same equation as
\tau
, hence it also defines the hypersurface
H
in
\mathbb{P}(E^{\prime})
.

Finally the line bundle
M
is replaced by
M^{\prime}:=M\otimes L^{d}
, and consequently the class
m
is replaced by:

m^{\prime}:=c_{1}(M^{\prime})=m+d\textrm{ }c_{1}(L).

Observe that this implies the equality of classes in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(N+1)m^{\prime}-de^{\prime}=(N+1)m-de.

We shall denote the relative canonical sheaf of the smooth morphism

\pi:\mathbb{P}(E)\longrightarrow C

by:

(6.2.10)

\omega_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}\simeq\Omega^{N}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}\simeq\det T_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}^{\vee}.

Definition and Proposition 6.2.2
.

Let
h^{\ast}\in{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}(E))_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the class defined by:

h^{\ast}:=-\frac{1}{N+1}c_{1}(\omega_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}),

and let
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
be the rational number defined by:

{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C):=\int_{\mathbb{P}(E)}h^{\ast N}\cap[H].

They satisfy the following relations:

(6.2.11)

h^{\ast}=h+\frac{1}{N+1}\pi^{\ast}e,

(6.2.12)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}(E)}h^{N}\cap[H]+\frac{dN}{N+1}\deg E,

(6.2.13)

\displaystyle=\deg M-\frac{d}{N+1}\deg E.

In particular, the rational number
(N+1){\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
is an integer.

Proof.

The equality (
6.2.11
) comes from taking the determinant of the exact sequence (
6.2.1
). This gives isomorphisms:

\det T_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C}\simeq\det\big(\pi^{\ast}E\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(1)\big)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(N+1)\otimes\pi^{\ast}\det E.

Together with (
6.2.10
), this establishes (
6.2.11
).

By using successively (
6.2.9
), (
6.2.5
) and (
6.2.9
) again, we have:

\displaystyle h^{\ast N}\cap[H]

\displaystyle=\left(h+\frac{1}{N+1}\pi^{\ast}e\right)^{N}\cap[H],

\displaystyle=\left(h^{N}+\frac{N}{N+1}h^{N-1}\pi^{\ast}e\right)\cap[H],

\displaystyle=h^{N}\cap[H]+\frac{N}{N+1}h^{N-1}\pi^{\ast}e\textrm{ }.\textrm{ }(dh+\pi^{\ast}m),

(6.2.14)

\displaystyle=h^{N}\cap[H]+\frac{dN}{N+1}h^{N}\pi^{\ast}e.

After pushing forward by
\pi
and taking the degree, using (
6.2.6
), this becomes equality (
6.2.12
).

From (
6.2.14
) and (
6.2.5
), we obtain:

\displaystyle h^{\ast N}\cap[H]

\displaystyle=h^{N}.\textrm{ }(dh+\pi^{\ast}m)+\frac{dN}{N+1}h^{N}\pi^{\ast}e,

\displaystyle=dh^{N+1}+h^{N}\pi^{\ast}m+\frac{dN}{N+1}h^{N}\pi^{\ast}e,

so pushing it forward by
\pi
and using (
6.2.6
), we get:

\pi_{\ast}(h^{\ast N}\cap[H])=-de+m+\frac{dN}{N+1}e=m-\frac{d}{N+1}e.

Consequently, we have:

\int_{\mathbb{P}(E)}h^{\ast N}\cap[H]=\deg(\pi_{\ast}(h^{\ast N}\cap[H]))=\deg M-\frac{d}{N+1}\deg E.

This shows equality (
6.2.13
).
∎

Remark 6.2.3
.

The subscript stands for “intersection-theoretic.” The height
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
clearly only depends on
\mathbb{P}(E)
as a scheme over
C
and on its subscheme
H
. We can also check this property on the expression (
6.2.13
): according to Remark
6.2.1
,
if
E
is replaced by
E^{\prime}:=E\otimes L
, with
L
a line bundle, the class
m-\frac{d}{N+1}e
is unchanged.

We can also express this height in terms of slopes. The slope of the line bundle
M
is
\mu(M)=\deg M
, the slope of
E
is:

\mu(E)=\frac{\deg E}{N+1},

and the one of
S^{d}E^{\vee}
is:

\mu(S^{d}E^{\vee})=-d\,\mu(E)=-\frac{d\deg E}{N+1},

which gives the following expression:

(6.2.15)

{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)=\mu(S^{d}E^{\vee})-\mu(M^{\vee}).

The existence of an effective section of the line bundle
M^{\otimes(N+1)(d-1)^{N}}\otimes(\det E)^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{N}}
on
C
defined by the discriminant of the horizontal hypersurface
H
(see (
6.2.33
) below), along with equality (
6.2.13
), will prove that the rational number
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
is non-negative when
d\geq 2
.

When the vector bundle
S^{d}E^{\vee}
is semistable, the non-negativity of
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
would follow from equation (
6.2.15
). Indeed, since
M^{\vee}
is naturally embedded as a coherent subsheaf of
S^{d}E^{\vee}
, using the expression (
6.2.15
) for
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
, if the vector bundle
S^{d}E^{\vee}
is stable (resp. semistable), then
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
is positive (resp. non-negative).

Combining this result with the compatibility of vector bundle stability with tensor operations in characteristic 0 (see for instance
[
NS65
]
), we immediately obtain that if the vector bundle
E
is semistable, then
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
is non-negative.

The non-negativity of
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
when the vector bundle
E
is semistable would also follow from
[
Miy87
, Th. 3.1]
: the vector bundle
E
is semistable if and only if the class
h^{\ast}
(which Miyaoka denotes by
\lambda_{\mathcal{E}}
) is nef, and when this holds, the rational number

{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C):=\int_{\mathbb{P}(E)}h^{\ast N}\cap[H]

is non-negative.

6.2.2.
The Griffiths height of the middle-dimensional cohomology of a pencil of hypersurfaces in projective spaces

The objective of this subsection is to show the following theorem, which immediately implies Theorem
1.4.2
using equality (
6.2.13
).
As observed after the statement of Theorem
1.4.2
, this theorem implies the validity of the same formulas for the Griffiths heights of the primitive part of the middle-dimensional cohomology
\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})
.

Theorem 6.2.4
.

Let
C
be a connected smooth projective complex curve with generic point
\eta
,
E
a vector bundle of rank
N+1
over
C
, and
H\subset\mathbb{P}(E)
an horizontal hypersurface of relative degree
d
, smooth over
\mathbb{C}.

If
\pi_{\mid H}
has only a finite number of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate, then the reduced
0
-cycle in
H
defined by the set of critical points
\Sigma
satisfies the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)
:

(6.2.16)

f_{\ast}[\Sigma]=(d-1)^{N}((N+1)m-de).

Moreover, we have the following equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.2.17)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=F_{+}(d,N)(m-\frac{d}{N+1}e),

and:

(6.2.18)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=F_{-}(d,N)(m-\frac{d}{N+1}e),

and the following equality of rational numbers:

(6.2.19)

\mathrm{ht}_{GK,stab}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta}))=F_{stab}(d,N)\,\mathrm{ht}_{int}(H/C),

where
F_{+}(d,N)
,
F_{-}(d,N)
and
F_{stab}(d,N)
are the elements of
(1/12)\mathbb{Z}
given when
N
is odd by:

F_{+}(d,N):=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\left[(d-1)^{N}(7d^{2}N-7d^{2}-2dN-2)+2(d^{2}-1)\right],

F_{-}(d,N):=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\left[(d-1)^{N}(-5d^{2}N+5d^{2}-2dN-2)+2(d^{2}-1)\right],

and:

F_{stab}(d,N):=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\left[(d-1)^{N}(d^{2}N-d^{2}-2dN-2)+2(d^{2}-1)\right],

and, when
N
is even, by:

F_{+}(d,N)=F_{-}(d,N)=F_{stab}(d,N):=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\left[(d-1)^{N}(d^{2}N+2d^{2}-2dN-2)-2(d^{2}-1)\right].

Observe that the morphism of complex schemes:

\pi:X:=\mathbb{P}(E)\longrightarrow C

is smooth and surjective, and using the isomorphism
6.2.2
, the line bundle
L
is relatively ample. Consequently, we are in the situation of Section
6.1
.

Proposition 6.2.5
.

With the above notation, and denoting
L
the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}(E)}(H)
on
\mathbb{P}(E)
, we have the equalities in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(\mathbb{P}(E))
:

(6.2.20)

i_{\ast}[\Sigma]=(d-1)^{N}h^{N}[(d-1)h+(N+1)\pi^{\ast}m-\pi^{\ast}e],

(6.2.21)

c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})=(-1)^{N}h^{N}[d(N+1)h+(N+1)\pi^{\ast}m+dN\pi^{\ast}e],

and:

(6.2.22)

[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})]^{(N+1)}=h^{N}(a_{N,d}h+b_{N,d}\pi^{\ast}m+c_{N,d}\pi^{\ast}e),

with the rational numbers
a_{N,d}
,
b_{N,d}
and
c_{N,d}
given by:

a_{N,d}:=\frac{N+1}{d}\big(-(d-1)^{N+1}+(-1)^{N+1}\big),

b_{N,d}:=\frac{N+1}{d^{2}}\big(-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big),

c_{N,d}:=\frac{1}{d}\big(-(d-1)^{N}(d-N-2)+(-1)^{N+1}(N+2)\big).

Proof.

Applying equality (
6.1.10
) from Proposition
6.1.4
with the above notation yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(\mathbb{P}(E))
:

\displaystyle i_{\ast}[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=\big[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big]^{(N+1)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N+1}\big[(1+c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(T_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big]^{(N+1)}

(6.2.23)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N+1}\big[(1+dh+\pi^{\ast}m)^{-1}c(\pi^{\ast}E\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(1))\big]^{(N+1)}

(6.2.24)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N+1}\Big[\big((1+dh)^{-1}-\pi^{\ast}m\,(1+dh)^{-2}\big)\big((1+h)^{N+1}+\pi^{\ast}e\,(1+h)^{N}\big)\Big]^{(N+1)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N+1}[(1+dh)^{-1}(1+h)^{N+1}]^{(N+1)}-(-1)^{N+1}\pi^{\ast}m\,[(1+dh)^{-2}(1+h)^{N+1}]^{(N)}

(6.2.25)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{N+1}\pi^{\ast}e\,[(1+dh)^{-1}(1+h)^{N}]^{(N)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N+1}[(-1)^{N+1}(d-1)^{N+1}]h^{N+1}-(-1)^{N+1}\pi^{\ast}m\,[(-1)^{N}(N+1)(d-1)^{N}]h^{N}

(6.2.26)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{N+1}\pi^{\ast}e\,[(-1)^{N}(d-1)^{N}]h^{N}

\displaystyle=(d-1)^{N}h^{N}[(d-1)h+(N+1)\pi^{\ast}m-\pi^{\ast}e],

where in (
6.2.23
), we used exact sequence (
6.2.1
) and equality (
6.2.5
); in (
6.2.24
), we used (
6.2.9
) and
[
Ful98
, Ex. 3.2.2]
; in (
6.2.25
), we used again (
6.2.9
); and in (
6.2.26
), we used Proposition
5.3.1
.

This shows (
6.2.20
). The other equalities are proved similarly with straightforward computations.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2.4
.

Pushing equality (
6.2.20
) forward by the morphism
\pi
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(C)
:

\displaystyle f_{\ast}[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=(d-1)^{N}[(d-1)\pi_{\ast}h^{N+1}+(N+1)\pi_{\ast}(h^{N}\pi^{\ast}m)-\pi_{\ast}(h^{N}\pi^{\ast}e)],

\displaystyle=(d-1)^{N}[-(d-1)e+(N+1)m-e]\textrm{ using the projection formula and \eqref{Segre class of E},}

\displaystyle=(d-1)^{N}[(N+1)m-de],

which shows equality (
6.2.16
).

Let us show equality (
6.2.17
). Since
X=\mathbb{P}(E)
is a projective bundle over
C
, all its relative Hodge vector bundles on
C
are trivial, and for every integer
n
, the Griffiths line bundle
\mathcal{GK}_{C}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/C))
is trivial. Since the line bundle
L
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
, we may apply the equality (
6.1.2
) established in Proposition
6.1.1
. With the above notation, this
yields the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

(6.2.27)

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\Big[\big((1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big)^{(N+1)}\Big]\\
-\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\big(c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big)+v_{N}^{+}f_{\ast}[\Sigma].

Using (
6.2.22
), the first term in (
6.2.27
) is given by:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\Big[\big((1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})c(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big)^{(N+1)}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}(a_{N,d}h^{N+1}+b_{N,d}h^{N}\pi^{\ast}m+c_{N,d}h^{N}\pi^{\ast}e),

(6.2.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}b_{N,d}m+\frac{1}{12}(c_{N,d}-a_{N,d})e,

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big[-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big]m

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{12d}\big[-(d-1)^{N}(d-N-2)+(-1)^{N+1}(N+2)+(N+1)(d-1)^{N+1}-(N+1)(-1)^{N+1}\big]e,

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big[-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big]m-\frac{1}{12d}\big[-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big]e,

(6.2.29)

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big(-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big)\big(m-\frac{d}{N+1}e\big),

where in (
6.2.28
), we have used the projection formula and (
6.2.6
).

Reasoning similarly using (
6.2.21
), the second term in (
6.2.27
) is given by:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\pi_{\ast}\big(c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}(E)/C})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N}}{12}\pi_{\ast}(d(N+1)h^{N+1}+(N+1)h^{N}\pi^{\ast}m+dNh^{N}\pi^{\ast}e),

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N}}{12}(-d(N+1)e+(N+1)m+dNe),

(6.2.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N}(N+1)}{12}\big(m-\frac{d}{N+1}e\big).

Replacing (
6.2.16
), (
6.2.29
) and (
6.2.30
) in (
6.2.27
) yields the equality:

c_{1}(\mathcal{GK}_{C,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/C_{\eta})))=F_{+}(d,N)\big(m-\frac{d}{N+1}e\big),

where
F_{+}(d,N)
is the rational number given by:

F_{+}(d,N)=\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big(-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+(-1)^{N}\big)-\frac{(-1)^{N}(N+1)}{12}+v_{N}^{+}(N+1)(d-1)^{N}.

If the integer
N
is odd,
F_{+}(d,N)
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{+}(d,N)

\displaystyle=-\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big((d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+1\big)+\frac{N+1}{12}+\frac{7(N-1)}{24}(N+1)(d-1)^{N},

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\Big[-2\big((d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+1\big)+2d^{2}+7d^{2}(N-1)(d-1)^{N}\Big],

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\Big[(d-1)^{N}(7d^{2}N-7d^{2}-2dN-2)+2(d^{2}-1)\Big],

as wanted, and if the integer
N
is even, it is given by:

\displaystyle F_{+}(d,N)

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{12d^{2}}\big(-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+1\big)-\frac{N+1}{12}+\frac{N+2}{24}(N+1)(d-1)^{N},

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\Big[2\big(-(d-1)^{N}(dN+1)+1\big)-2d^{2}+d^{2}(N+2)(d-1)^{N}\Big],

\displaystyle=\frac{N+1}{24d^{2}}\Big[(d-1)^{N}(d^{2}N+2d^{2}-2dN-2)-2(d^{2}-1)\Big],

as wanted. This shows equality (
6.2.17
).

For equality (
6.2.18
), we reason similarly using (
6.1.3
).

For equality (
6.2.19
), it follows from equality (
6.2.16
) and the definition of the height
{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C)
that we have the following equality of integers:

|\Sigma|=(d-1)^{N}[(N+1)\deg(m)-d\deg(e)]=(N+1)(d-1)^{N}{\mathrm{ht}}_{int}(H/C).

Equality (
6.2.19
) is a simple consequence of this equality combined with equality (
2.4.6
) from Corollary
2.4.2
and with equality (
6.2.18
).
∎

6.2.3.
Complements: horizontal hypersurfaces in a projective bundle and discriminant

Equality (
6.2.16
) from Theorem
6.2.4
can also be deduced from the classical theory of the discriminant, which we shall now recall.

Recall the definition (
[
Dem12
]
) of the discriminant of a homogeneous polynomial in an arbitrary number of indeterminates.

Let us denote by
n\geq 1
the number of indeterminates and by
d
the degree of the homogeneous polynomial. Let:

I_{n,d}:=\left\{\alpha=(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}=d\right\}

be the set of indexes, let:

U_{n,d}:=\mathbb{Z}\left[(T_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in I_{n,d}}\right]

be the universal polynomial ring, and let:

P_{n,d}:=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in I_{n,d}}X_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}...X_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}\,T_{\alpha}\in U_{n,d}[X_{1},...,X_{n}]

be the
universal homogeneous polynomial
of degree
d
in
n
indeterminates.
It satisfies the following tautological property.

For
R
a ring, let us denote by
R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}
the submodule of
R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]
of homogeneous polynomials of degree
d
. For every polynomial
F\in R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}
, there is a unique morphism of rings:

h_{F}:U_{n,d}\longrightarrow R,

such that the polynomial
h_{F}(P_{n,d})
deduced from
P_{n,d}
by replacing its coefficients in
U_{n,d}
by their images by
h_{F}
— in other terms, its base change by
h_{F}
— is
F
itself.

Definition and Proposition 6.2.6

(
[
Dem12
, 5]
; see also
[
GKZ08
, Chapter 13]
)
.

The
universal discriminant

{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(P_{n,d})
is an element of
U_{n,d}
given by:

{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(P_{n,d}):=d^{-a(n,d)}{\rm Res}\left(\frac{\partial P_{n,d}}{\partial X_{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial P_{n,d}}{\partial_{X_{n}}}\right),

where
{\rm Res}
denotes the resultant of
n
polynomials in
n
indeterminates, and the integer
a(n,d)
is defined by:

a(n,d):=\frac{(d-1)^{n}-(-1)^{n}}{d}.

For every ring
R
, if
F
is a polynomial in
R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}
, if
h_{F}:U_{n,d}\rightarrow R
denotes the morphism of rings such that
h_{F}(P_{n,d})=F
, then:

{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F):=h_{F}({\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(P_{n,d}))\in R

defines the
discriminant
of F.

The universal discriminant
{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(P_{n,d})
is characterized (up to a sign) by the following two properties:

(1)

It is a prime element of
U_{n,d}
.
2
2

2

see
[
Dem12
, 6, Cor. 1]
.

(2)

If
k
is a field, and
F
is a polynomial in
k[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}
, then
{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F)=0
if and only if the subscheme
(F=0)
in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{k}
is not smooth.
3
3

3

see
[
Dem12
, Prop. 12]
.

Moreover, for every ring
R
and for every homogeneous polynomial
F
in
R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}
,
the following equalities hold in
U_{n,d}:
4
4

4

see
[
Dem12
, Prop. 11, c) and e)]
.

(3)

If
\gamma
is a scalar in
R
, then we have:

(6.2.31)

{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(\gamma F)=\gamma^{n(d-1)^{n-1}}{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F).

(4)

If
A=(a_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
is a matrix with entries in
R
, then the discriminant of the polynomial
F^{\prime}:=F\left(\sum\limits_{j}a_{1,j}X_{j},\sum\limits_{j}a_{2,j}X_{j},...\right)
is given by:

(6.2.32)

{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F^{\prime})=(\det A)^{d(d-1)^{n-1}}{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F).

We can extend this definition to families of polynomials parametrised by a scheme in the following way.

Definition and Proposition 6.2.7
.

Let
X
be a scheme,
M
be a line bundle over
X
,
E
be a vector bundle of rank
n
on
X
, and let
\tau
be a section over
X
of the vector bundle
S^{d}E^{\vee}\otimes M
.

There exists a unique section
{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(\tau)
over
X
of the line bundle:

M^{\otimes n(d-1)^{n-1}}\otimes(\det E)^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{n-1}}

satisfying the following property:

(1)

If
V={\rm Spec\,}R
is an affine open subset of
X
, if

\varphi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M_{V},\quad\psi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus n}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}E_{V}

are local trivializations of the line bundle
M
and the vector bundle
E
, then the scalar

\left((\varphi^{-1})^{\otimes n(d-1)^{n-1}}\otimes(\det\psi^{-1})^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{n-1}}\right)({\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(\tau))\in\Gamma(V,{\mathcal{O}}_{V})\simeq R,

is precisely the discriminant
{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(F)
of the polynomial

F:=\left((S^{d}\ {}^{t}\psi)\otimes\varphi^{-1}\right)(\tau_{|V})\in\Gamma(V,S^{d}{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus n})\simeq R[X_{1},...,X_{n}]_{d}.

This section also satisfies the following property:

(2)

If
x
is a point in
X
with residue field
\kappa(x)
, then the section
{\rm{disc}}_{n,d}(\tau)
vanishes at
x
if and only if the hypersurface in
\mathbb{P}(E_{\kappa(x)})
defined by the vanishing of the polynomial
\tau(x)\in S^{d}E_{\kappa(x)}^{\vee}\otimes M_{\kappa(x)}
is not smooth over
\kappa(x)
.

The proof is straightforward.

Consider now a connected smooth projective complex curve
C
with generic point
\eta
, a vector bundle
E
of rank
N+1
over
C,
and a horizontal hypersurface
H\subset\mathbb{P}(E)
such that
H_{\eta}
is smooth over
\eta
, and let us adopt the notation of the previous subsections.

In particular, we denote by
\tau
a section of
S^{d}E^{\vee}\otimes M
representing the “equation” of
H
.

By applying to
\tau
the construction in the previous proposition, we get a section:

(6.2.33)

{\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)\in\Gamma\left(C,M^{\otimes(N+1)(d-1)^{N}}\otimes(\det E)^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{N}}\right).

The property
2
of Definition-Proposition
6.2.7
implies that for every scheme point
x
in
C
, the section
{\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)
vanishes at
x
if and only if the hypersurface
H_{\kappa(x)}\subset\mathbb{P}(E_{\kappa(x)})
is singular. In particular,
{\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)
is not the zero section of
M^{\otimes(N+1)(d-1)^{N}}\otimes(\det E)^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{N}}
.

Let
\Delta
be the finite subset of critical values in
C
of the morphism

f:H\longrightarrow C.

Thanks to the work of Eriksson (
[
Eri16
, Th. 1.2]
), one may express the order of vanishing of the section
{\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)
at some point
x
in
\Delta
in terms of the localized
N
-th Chern class of the coherent sheaf
\Omega^{1}_{H/C}
, which is supported on the singular fibers of
H
.

In the special case where
H
is smooth and the morphism
f
has only non-degenerate critical points, his result easily implies the equality, for every
x
in
\Delta
:

\mathrm{ord}_{x}\big({\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)\big)=|\Sigma_{x}|,

where
\Sigma
denotes the set of critical points of
f
and:

\Sigma_{x}:=\Sigma\cap f^{-1}(x).

This implies the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(C)
:

\displaystyle f_{\ast}[\Sigma]:=\sum_{x\in\Delta}|\Sigma_{x}|\,x

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\Delta}\mathrm{ord}_{x}({\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau))\,x,

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div}\big({\rm{disc}}_{N+1,d}(\tau)\big),

\displaystyle=c_{1}\big(M^{\otimes(N+1)(d-1)^{N}}\otimes(\det E)^{\otimes-d(d-1)^{N}}\big),

\displaystyle=(d-1)^{N}\big((N+1)m-de\big),

and provides an alternative proof of (
6.2.16
).

6.3.
Linear pencils of hypersurfaces and Lefschetz pencils

Another setting where Proposition
6.1.1
applies is provided by linear pencils of hypersurfaces.

Let
V
be a connected smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N\geq 1
, and let
H
be a non-singular hypersurface in
V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
such that the morphism

\mathrm{pr}_{1|H}:H\longrightarrow V

is dominant, or equivalently surjective, and let
\delta
be its degree.
The line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(H)
is isomorphic to the line bundle

\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}M\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\delta),

where
M
denotes some line bundle over
V
, which is unique up to isomorphism.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

With the above notation,
let us assume that the morphism

\mathrm{pr}_{2|H}:H\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}

is surjective, and has a finite set
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate.

The following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(V\times\mathbb{P}^{1})
:

(6.3.1)

[\Sigma]=\delta\,\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}[(1-c_{1}(M))^{-2}c(\Omega^{1}_{V})]^{(N)}\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)).

In particular, the cardinality of
\Sigma
satisfies:

|\Sigma|=\delta\int_{V}(1-c_{1}(M))^{-2}c(\Omega^{1}_{V}).

Furthermore, if the line bundle
M
on
V
is ample, the following equalities of integers hold:

(6.3.2)

\deg_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{GK}_{\mathbb{P}^{1},+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\eta})))\\
=\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V}(1-c_{1}(M))^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}\delta}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{+}|\Sigma|,

and:

(6.3.3)

\deg_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{GK}_{\mathbb{P}^{1},-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\eta})))\\
=\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V}(1-c_{1}(M))^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}\delta}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{-}|\Sigma|,

where:

\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)=(-1)^{N}\int_{V}c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V})

denotes the topological Euler characteristic of
V
, and where
v_{N}^{+}
and
v_{N}^{-}
are the rational numbers defined in Proposition
6.1.1
.

We could have formulated (
6.3.2
) and (
6.3.3
) as equalities in the Chow group
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})_{\mathbb{Q}}
of the base
\mathbb{P}^{1}
of the pencil, as in Proposition
6.1.1
. However the degree map establishes an isomorphism
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\mathbb{Q}
, and the present formulation is actually equivalent to this
a priori
more precise one.

6.3.1.
Proof of Proposition
6.3.1

For simplicity, let us denote
h
the class
c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1))
in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
and
m
the class
c_{1}(M)
in
{\mathrm{CH}}^{1}(V)
.

We can apply equality (
6.1.1
) from Proposition
6.1.1
to the smooth projective complex scheme
X:=V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
; the smooth surjective morphism of complex schemes

\pi:=\mathrm{pr}_{2}:X\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}

and the line bundle on
X
:

L:={\mathcal{O}}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(H)\simeq\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}M\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\delta).

We obtain the equality in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(V\times\mathbb{P}^{1})
:

\displaystyle[\Sigma]

\displaystyle=\big[(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})\big]^{(N+1)}

\displaystyle=\big[(1-\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}m-\delta\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h)^{-1}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big]^{(N+1)}

(6.3.4)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\big[(1-m)^{-1}c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big]^{(N+1)}+\delta\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h\,\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\big[(1-m)^{-2}c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big]^{(N)}

(6.3.5)

\displaystyle=\delta\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h\,\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\big[(1-m)^{-2}.c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big]^{(N)},

where in (
6.3.4
), we have used that
{\mathrm{CH}}^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
vanishes, and in (
6.3.5
), we have used that
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N+1}(V)
vanishes.

This shows (
6.3.1
).

Now, let us assume that the line bundle
M
on
V
is ample. This implies that the line bundle
L
on
X
is ample relatively to the morphism
\pi
, so that the hypothesis of (
6.1.2
) and (
6.1.3
) is satisfied.

Since
X
is the product of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
by
V
, all its relative Hodge vector bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
are trivial, and for every integer
n
, the Griffiths line bundle
\mathcal{GK}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathbb{H}^{n}(X/\mathbb{P}^{1}))
is trivial.

Consequently, taking degrees in equality (
6.1.2
) yields the following equality of rational numbers:

(6.3.6)

\deg_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{GK}_{\mathbb{P}^{1},+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\eta})))=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})c(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})\\
-\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}))+v_{N}^{+}|\Sigma|.

Reasoning as above, the first term in (
6.3.6
) can be rewritten:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1-c_{1}(L))^{-1}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})c(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1-\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}m-\delta\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h)^{-1}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\big(c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}(1-m)^{-1}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\big(c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h\,\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}(1-m)^{-2}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}(c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})),

(6.3.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V}(1-m)^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V}),

where we have used the fact that the group
{\mathrm{CH}}^{N+1}(V)
vanishes, as well as the classical equality, for
\alpha
a
0
-cycle in
V
:

\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}\alpha\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h=\int_{V}\alpha.

Similarly, the second term in (
6.3.6
) can be rewritten:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}c_{1}(L)c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{P}^{1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}m+\delta\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h)\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V}),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}(m\,c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V}))+\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}h\,\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V}),

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V}c_{N}(\Omega^{1}_{V}),

(6.3.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{N}\delta}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V).

Replacing (
6.3.7
) and (
6.3.8
) in (
6.3.6
) yields the equality:

\deg_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{GK}_{\mathbb{P}^{1},+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(H_{\eta}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\eta})))=\frac{\delta}{12}\int_{V}(1-m)^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}\delta}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{+}|\Sigma|,

which shows equality (
6.3.2
). Equality (
6.3.3
) follows similarly from equality (
6.1.3
).

6.3.2.
Application to Lefschetz pencils

Proposition
6.3.1
applies notably to Lefschetz pencils.

Let
V
be a connected smooth projective complex scheme of pure dimension
N\geq 1
, embedded into some projective space
\mathbb{P}^{r}
, of dimension
r\geq\max(N,2).

Let
\Lambda
be a projective subspace of dimension
r-2
in
\mathbb{P}^{r}
that intersects
V
transversally, and let
P\subset\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}
the projective line in the dual projective space
\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}
corresponding to
\Lambda
by projective duality.

Let us denote by:

\nu:\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{\Lambda}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{r}

the blow-up of
\Lambda
in
\mathbb{P}^{r}
. If
\widetilde{V}
denote the proper transform of
V
by
\nu
, the restriction:

\nu_{\mid\widetilde{V}}:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow V

may be identified with the blow-up in
V
of
\Lambda\cap V
, which is smooth of dimension
r-2
.

Let
I
be the incidence subscheme in
\mathbb{P}^{r}\times\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}
. It is an hypersurface in
\mathbb{P}^{r}\times\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}
, and both projections:

\mathrm{pr}_{1\mid I}:I\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{r}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathrm{pr}_{2\mid I}:I\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}

are smooth morphisms. Moreover the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}(I)
over
\mathbb{P}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r\vee}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{r}}(1)\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{r\vee}}(1).

The projection of center
\Lambda
:

\mathbb{P}^{r}-\Lambda\longrightarrow P

extends to a smooth morphism of complex schemes:

p:\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{\Lambda}\longrightarrow P,

and the pair of morphisms
(\nu,p)
establishes an isomorphism:

(\nu,p):\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{\Lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}I\cap(\mathbb{P}^{r}\times P)

between the scheme
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{\Lambda}
and the smooth hypersurface
I\cap(\mathbb{P}^{r}\times P)
of
\mathbb{P}^{r}\times P
.

Consequently, by restriction, the pair of morphisms
(\nu_{\mid\widetilde{V}},p_{\mid\widetilde{V}})
defines an isomorphism:

(\nu_{\mid\widetilde{V}},p_{\mid\widetilde{V}}):\widetilde{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}I\cap(V\times P)

between
\widetilde{V}
and the smooth hypersurface
I\cap(V\times P)
of
V\times P
. Moreover the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}\big(I\cap(V\times P)\big)
over
V\times P
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{r}}(1)_{\mid V}\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{P}(1).

Recall that the pencil of hyperplanes in
\mathbb{P}^{r}
containing
\Lambda
is said to be a Lefschetz pencil with respect to the subvariety
V
of
\mathbb{P}^{r}
when the morphism:

p_{\mid\widetilde{V}}:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow P

has a finite set
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate, and when the restriction

p_{|\Sigma}:\Sigma\longrightarrow P

is an injective map.

We may apply Proposition
6.3.1
to the hypersurface
I\cap(V\times P)
in
V\times P
. With the notation of this proposition in this situation, the line bundle
M
is (isomorphic to) the restriction
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{r}}(1)
to
V
, and
\delta=1
. Accordingly, we obtain the following result, which notably applies to Lefschetz pencils:

Corollary 6.3.2
.

With the above notation, let us assume that the morphism:

p_{\mid\widetilde{V}}:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow P

has a finite set
\Sigma
of critical points, all of which are non-degenerate.

The following equality holds in
{\mathrm{CH}}_{0}(V\times P)
:

(\nu,p)_{\ast}[\Sigma]=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{\ast}[(1-c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)))^{-2}c(\Omega^{1}_{V})]^{(N)}\,\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\ast}c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{P}(1)).

In particular, the cardinality of
\Sigma
satisfies:

(6.3.9)

|\Sigma|=\int_{V}(1-c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)))^{-2}c(\Omega^{1}_{V}).

Furthermore, the following equalities of integers hold:

\deg_{P}(\mathcal{GK}_{P,+}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\eta}/P_{\eta})))\\
=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V}(1-c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)))^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{+}|\Sigma|,

and:

\deg_{P}(\mathcal{GK}_{P,-}(\mathbb{H}^{N-1}(\widetilde{V}_{\eta}/P_{\eta})))\\
=\frac{1}{12}\int_{V}(1-c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}(1)))^{-2}c_{1}(\Omega^{1}_{V})c(\Omega^{1}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{12}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(V)+v_{N}^{-}|\Sigma|.

As already mentioned in
1.4.4
, the expression (
6.3.9
) for the number of critical points in a Lefschetz pencil is established by Katz in
[
SGA73
, Exposé XVII, cor. 5.6]
.
